\displaystyle\leq 2d^{1-\frac{\pi}{4}}\inf_{t\geq 0}\sup_{\theta\in[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\exp(-nt\varepsilon)[\mathbf{vec}(\bm{I}_{d})]^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[\prod_{i=n}^{1}\exp(t\bm{H}_{i}(s_{i},\theta))\mathbf{vec}(\bm{I}_{d})\right]

Here, we use
\mathbb{E}_{s}
to denote the expectation taken over all the samples in the Markov chain
s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{n}
, where
s_{1}\sim\mu
and
s_{i+1}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{i})
for all
1\leq i<n
. In order to bound this expectation, we define, for any
t>0
and
\theta\in[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}]
, a sequence of vector-valued functions
\{\bm{g}_{k}\}_{k\in[n]}
taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
, where

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{0}(x)=\textbf{vec}(\bm{I}_{d}),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k}(x)=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=k}^{1}\exp(t\bm{H}_{i}(s_{i},\theta))\textbf{vec}(\bm{I}_{d})\bigg|s_{k+1}=x\right],\quad k\geq 1.

In this way, the left-hand-side of (
12
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{s_{1}\sim\mu}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{F}_{i}(s_{i})\right\|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2d^{1-\frac{\pi}{4}}\inf_{t\geq 0}\sup_{\theta\in[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\exp(-nt\varepsilon)[\mathbf{vec}(\bm{I}_{d})]^{\top}\mu(\bm{g}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq 2d^{1-\frac{\pi}{4}}\inf_{t\geq 0}\sup_{\theta\in[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\exp(-nt\varepsilon)\left\|\mathbf{vec}(\bm{I}_{d})\right\|_{2}\cdot\|\mu(\bm{g}_{n})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=2d^{1-\frac{\pi}{4}}\inf_{t\geq 0}\sup_{\theta\in[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\exp(-nt\varepsilon)\sqrt{d}\cdot\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}

where we applied the fact that
s_{n+1}\sim\mu
when
s_{1}\sim\mu
. Recall that by definition,
\mu(\bm{g}_{n})=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mu}[\bm{g}_{n}(x)]
and
\bm{g}_{n}^{\parallel}
represents the constant function taking this value.
Next, we construct a recursive relation among the sequence
\{\bm{g}_{k}\}_{k\in[n]}
for the purpose of bounding the norm of
\bm{g}_{n}
. Notice that, on one hand,

\displaystyle\exp\left(t\bm{H}_{k}(x,\theta)\right)\bm{g}_{k-1}(x)=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=k}^{1}\exp(t\bm{H}_{i}(s_{i}))\bigg|s_{k}=x\right];

on the other hand,

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=k}^{1}\exp(t\bm{H}_{i}(s_{i},\theta))\textbf{vec}(\bm{I}_{d})\bigg|s_{k+1}=x\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{y\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=k}^{1}\exp(t\bm{H}_{i}(s_{i},\theta))\textbf{vec}(\bm{I}_{d})\bigg|s_{k}=y\right]\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(s_{k}=y\mid s_{k+1}=x)

\displaystyle=\int_{y\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=k}^{1}\exp(t\bm{H}_{i}(s_{i},\theta))\textbf{vec}(\bm{I}_{d})\bigg|s_{k}=y\right]\right\}P^{*}(x,\mathrm{d}y);

here the contents in the curly bracket is equal to
\exp\left(t\bm{H}_{k}(y,\theta)\right)\bm{g}_{k-1}(u)
. Therefore, the sequence
\{\bm{g}_{k}\}
is featured by the recursive relation

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{y\in\mathcal{S}}\exp\left(t\bm{H}_{k}(y,\theta)\right)\bm{g}_{k-1}(y)P^{*}(x,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{P}^{*}(\exp(t\bm{H}_{k})\bm{g}_{k-1}))(x).

(56)

Based on this recursive relationship, the following proposition comes in handy for bounding the norm of
\bm{g}_{k}
recursively.

Proposition B.1
.

For any matrix function
\bm{g}:\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
and any bounded symmetric matrix function
\bm{H}:\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{S}^{m\times m}
with
\mu(\bm{H})=\bm{0}
and
\|\bm{F}(x)\|\leq M
almost surely. Then the following hold for any
t>0
:

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\parallel}\right)^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu}\leq\alpha_{1}\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu},\quad\text{where}\quad\alpha_{1}=\exp(tM)-tM;

(57)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\parallel}\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}\leq\alpha_{2}\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu},\quad\text{where}\quad\alpha_{2}=\lambda(\exp(tM)-1);

(58)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\perp}\right)^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu}\leq\alpha_{3}\|\bm{g}^{\perp}\|_{\mu},\quad\text{where}\quad\alpha_{3}=\exp(tM)-1;

(59)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\perp}\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}\leq\alpha_{4}\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu},\quad\text{where}\quad\alpha_{4}=\lambda\exp(tM).

(60)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.1
.
∎

A recursive relation can be constructed to bound the norm of
\bm{g}_{n}^{\parallel}
. Specifically, for every
k\in[n]
, (
56
),the triangle inequality and (
57
), (
58
) guarantee that

\displaystyle\|\bm{g}_{k}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\left\|(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H}_{k})\bm{g}_{k-1})^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H}_{k})\bm{g}_{k-1}^{\parallel})^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu}+\left\|(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H}_{k}))\bm{g}_{k-1}^{\perp})^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{1k}\|\bm{g}_{k-1}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}+\alpha_{2k}\|\bm{g}_{k-1}^{\perp}\|_{\mu};

here, we define
\alpha_{1k}=\exp(\frac{4}{\pi}tM_{k})-\frac{4}{\pi}tM_{k}
and
\alpha_{2k}=\lambda(\exp(\frac{4}{\pi}tM_{k})-1)
. In order to iteratively bound the norm of
\bm{g}_{k}^{\parallel}
, we also need to bound the norm of
\bm{g}_{k-1}^{\perp}
. Towards this end, we observe, due to (
56
), the triangle inequality and (
59
), (
60
) that for every
k\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{g}_{k}^{\perp}\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\left\|(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H}_{k}))\bm{g}_{k-1})^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H}_{k}))\bm{g}_{k-1}^{\parallel})^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}+\left\|(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H}_{k}))\bm{g}_{k-1}^{\perp})^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{3k}\|\bm{g}_{k-1}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}+\alpha_{4k}\|\bm{g}_{k-1}^{\perp}\|_{\mu}.

Here again, we define
\alpha_{3k}=\exp(\frac{4}{\pi}tM_{k})-1
and
\alpha_{4k}=\lambda\exp(\frac{4}{\pi}tM_{k})
. For simplicity, we denote

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}\|\bm{g}_{0}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}\\
\|\bm{g}_{0}^{\perp}\|_{\mu}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{d}\\
0\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{k}=\underset{\bm{U}_{j}}{\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1k}&\alpha_{3k}\\
\alpha_{3k}&\alpha_{4k}\end{pmatrix}}}\bm{x}_{k-1},\quad\forall k\in[n].

It can then be easily verified through an induction argument that

\displaystyle\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}\leq x_{n1}.

Notice here that we applied the fact
\alpha_{2k}<\alpha_{3k}
, since
\lambda<1
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq x_{n1}\leq\|\bm{x}_{n}\|_{2}=\left\|\prod_{k=1}^{n}\bm{U}_{k}\bm{x}_{0}\right\|_{2}\leq\prod_{k=1}^{n}\|\bm{U}_{k}\|\|\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}=\sqrt{d}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{n}\|\bm{U}_{k}\|.

Since
\bm{U}_{k}
is a symmetric
2\times 2
matrix, its operator norm is featured by

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{k}\|=\max\{|\sigma_{k1}|,|\sigma_{k2}|\}

where
\sigma_{k1}
and
\sigma_{k2}
are the two eigenvalues of
\bm{U}_{k}
. Recall from elementary linear algebra that
\sigma_{j1}
and
\sigma_{j2}
are solutions to the equation

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1k}-x)(\alpha_{4k}-x)-\alpha_{3k}^{2}=0.

Since
\alpha_{1k}>0
and
\alpha_{4k}>0
, it can be easily verified that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{k}\|=\frac{\alpha_{1k}+\alpha_{4k}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{(\alpha_{1k}-\alpha_{4k})^{2}+4\alpha_{3k}^{2}}}{2}.

The following lemma comes in handy for bounding the norm of
\bm{U}_{k}
.

Lemma B.2
.

For any
x>0
and
\lambda\in(0,1)
, denote
\alpha_{1}=e^{x}-x
,
\alpha_{3}=e^{x}-1
and
\alpha_{4}=\lambda e^{x}
. It can then be guaranteed that

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{4}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{4})^{2}+4\alpha_{3}^{2}}}{2}\leq\exp\left(\frac{5}{1-\lambda}x^{2}\right).

Proof.

See Appendix
D.2
.
∎

As a direct implication of lemma
B.2
, the norm of
\bm{U}_{k}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{k}\|\leq\exp\left(\frac{5}{1-\lambda}(\frac{4}{\pi}t)^{2}M_{k}^{2}\right);

consequently, the norm of
\bm{g}_{n}^{\parallel}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}\exp\left(\left(\frac{4}{\pi}\right)^{2}\frac{5t^{2}}{1-\lambda}\sum_{k=1}^{n}M_{k}^{2}\right)

for any
t\geq 0
and
\theta\in\left[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}\right]
. Therefore, the left-hand-side of (
12
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{s_{1}\sim\mu}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{F}_{i}(s_{i})\right\|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2d^{2-\frac{\pi}{4}}\inf_{t\geq 0}\exp(-nt\varepsilon)\exp\left(\left(\frac{4}{\pi}\right)^{2}\frac{5t^{2}}{1-\lambda}\sum_{k=1}^{n}M_{k}^{2}\right).

By letting

\displaystyle t=\frac{1-\lambda}{10}\left(\frac{\pi}{4}\right)^{2}\frac{n}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}M_{k}^{2}}\cdot\varepsilon,

we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{s_{1}\sim\mu}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{F}_{i}(s_{i})\right\|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2d^{2-\frac{\pi}{4}}\exp\left\{-\frac{1-\lambda}{20}\left(\frac{\pi}{4}\right)^{2}\frac{n^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}M_{k}^{2}}\right\}

which completes the analysis for the case that
\nu=\mu
. In order to extend this result to general
\nu
, we define functions
f(x)
and
g(x)
as

\displaystyle f(x):=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{F}_{i}(s_{i})\right\|\geq\varepsilon\Bigg|s_{1}=x\right),\quad\text{and}\quad g(x):=\frac{\nu(\mathrm{d}x)}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)}

respectively. Then by definition,
g(x)\geq 0
and
f(x)\in[0,1]
for all
x\in\mathcal{S}
. Therefore, the Holder’s inequality guarantees

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{s_{1}\sim\nu}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{F}_{i}(s_{i})\right\|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}}f(x)\nu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}}f(x)g(x)\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\|fg\|_{\mu,1}\leq\|f\|_{\mu,q}\|g\|_{\mu,p}=\|f\|_{\mu,q}\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p},

(61)

where, since
q>1
and
f(x)\in[0,1]
almost surely,
\|f\|_{\mu,q}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mu,q}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathcal{S}}f^{q}(x)\mathrm{d}\mu\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq\left(\int_{\mathcal{S}}f(x)\mathrm{d}\mu\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq\left[\mathbb{P}_{s_{1}\sim\mu}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{F}_{i}(s_{i})\right\|\geq\varepsilon\right)\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(62)

Theorem
3.5
follows immediately.

B.2
Proof of Theorem
3.3

This proof is a correction and improvement of Theorem 1 in
Srikant [2025]
, which applies Stein’s method and Lindeberg’s decomposition.
Using notation from
Anastasiou et al. [2019]
, we denote, for every
k\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bm{x}_{k},\quad\bm{T}_{k}=\sum_{j=k}^{n}\bm{V}_{j}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}_{j},\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{T}_{k}^{\prime}=\bm{T}_{k}+\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}^{\prime}.

Here,
\{\bm{z}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n}
and
\bm{z}^{\prime}
are
i.i.d.
standard Gaussian random variables in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and independent of the filtration
\{\mathscr{F}_{k}\}_{k=0}^{n}
. Note that since we assume
\bm{P}_{1}=n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
almost surely, and that
\{\bm{V}_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq k}
are all measurable with respect to
\mathscr{F}_{k-1}
, the matrices

\displaystyle\bm{P}_{k}=\bm{P}_{1}-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\bm{V}_{j}=n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\bm{V}_{j},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\bm{P}_{k+1}=\bm{P}_{1}-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bm{V}_{j}=n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bm{V}_{j}

are also measurable with respect to
\mathscr{F}_{k-1}
.
Next, since
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{T}_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{n})
, we have that

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{W}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bm{x}_{k},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{S}_{n}\right)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{T}_{1}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{T}_{1})]|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{T}_{1}^{\prime})]|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{T}_{1})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{T}_{1}^{\prime})]|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{T}_{1}^{\prime})]|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}}\mathbb{E}|h(\bm{T}_{1})-h(\bm{T}_{1}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{T}_{1}^{\prime})]|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{T}_{1}^{\prime})]|+\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma})}{n}},

(63)

where we have used Jensen’s inequality in steps (i) and (iii) and the Lipchitz property in (ii).
Using Lindeberg’s swapping and the triangle inequality, we obtain that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{T}_{1}^{\prime})]|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k-1}+\bm{T}_{k}^{\prime})]|,

(64)

where we define
\bm{S}_{0}=\bm{T}_{n+1}=0
for consistency. For each
k\in[n]
, define

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}_{k}(\bm{x}):=h(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}+\bm{S}_{k-1}),

where we denote, for simplicity,

\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime}=\bm{P}_{k}+\bm{\Sigma}.

Since
h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}
,
\widetilde{h}_{k}
has Lipchitz coefficient
L_{k}=\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime\frac{1}{2}}\|
. Let
f_{k}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}_{k}(\bm{x})-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{h}_{k}(\bm{z})]=\Delta f_{k}(\bm{x})-\bm{x}^{\top}\nabla f_{k}(\bm{x})

holds for any
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where
\bm{z}
is the
d
-dimensional standard Gaussian random variable;
f_{k}
is the solution to the multivariate Stein equation, and therefore well defined and measurable with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}

[see, e.g.,
Nourdin and Peccati, 2012
, Section 4.3]
. Therefore,

\displaystyle h(\bm{S}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k-1}+\bm{T}_{k}^{\prime})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle=h(\bm{S}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k-1}+\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{h}_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime}))-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{h}_{k}(\bm{z})]

\displaystyle=\Delta f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}))-\left[\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime})\right]^{\top}\nabla f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime})).

Notice that by definition,
\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{T}_{k+1}\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}})
. Below we let
\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k}
be a random vector such that
\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k}\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}})
and independent of
\bm{x}_{k}
conditionally on
\mathscr{F}_{k-1}
. By taking conditional expectations, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[h(\bm{S}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k-1}+\bm{T}_{k}^{\prime})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\Delta f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]-\mathbb{E}\left[(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})^{\top}\nabla f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right].

Since, by definition,
\Delta f_{k}=\mathsf{Tr}(\nabla^{2}f_{k})
, this can be further decomposed

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[h(\bm{S}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k-1}+\bm{T}_{k}^{\prime})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{1}}{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\right)-\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k}^{\top}\nabla f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]}}

\displaystyle+\underset{I_{2}}{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left(\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\right)\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]}}

\displaystyle-\underset{I_{3}}{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\nabla f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]}}-\underset{I_{4}}{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\left(\nabla f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\right)\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]}}.

(65)

Of the four terms on the right-hand-side,
I_{1}
is equal to
\bm{0}
according to Lemma 2 in
Srikant [2025]
;
I_{3}
is also
\bm{0}
according to martingale property. Below, we will handle
I_{2}
and
I_{4}
.

Controlling
I_{2}

We first make the observation that

\displaystyle\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}=\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime}-\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime})\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}=\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{k}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}};

Therefore, the term
I_{2}
can be represented as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left(\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\right)\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{k}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\right)\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathsf{Tr}\left\{\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{k}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}\left\{\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{k}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Tr}\left\{\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{k}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right\},

(66)

where we invoked the linearity of trace and expectation in (i).

Controlling
I_{4}

For clarity, we define a uni-dimensional function

\displaystyle F(t):=(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\nabla f_{k}(t\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k}),\quad\forall t\in[0,1].

Since
f_{k}
is twice-differentiable, the derivative of
F(t)
is given by

\displaystyle F^{\prime}(t)=(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(t\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}).

Consequently, the Lagrange’s mean-value theorem guarantees the existence of
\Theta\in[0,1]
, such that

\displaystyle(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\left(\nabla f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\right)

\displaystyle=F(1)-F(0)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}F^{\prime}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(t\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})\mathrm{d}t.

Consequently, the term
I_{4}
is characterized by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\left(\nabla f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\right)\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{1}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(t\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})\mathrm{d}t\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{1}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}(\nabla^{2}f_{k}(t\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k}))(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})\mathrm{d}t\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right],

(67)

where
\Theta\in(0,1)
. Here, the first term on the right-most part of the equation can be further computed by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left((\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})\right)\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Tr}\left((\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\right)\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\mathsf{Tr}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\bm{x}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{=}\mathsf{Tr}\left\{\mathbb{E}[\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\bm{x}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]\mathbb{E}[\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{=}\mathsf{Tr}\left\{\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{k}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]\right\};

(68)

notice that we applied the basic property of matrix trace in (i), the linearity of expectation in (ii), the conditional independence between
\bm{x}_{k}
and
\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k}
in (iii), and the definition of
\bm{V}_{k}
in (iv). The right-most part of this equation is exactly the same as the first term on the right-most part of (
66
).

Consequently, putting relations (
65
), (
66
), (
67
) and (
68
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[h(\bm{S}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k-1}+\bm{T}_{k}^{\prime})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}\left\{\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{k}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{1}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}(\nabla^{2}f_{k}(t\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k}))(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})\mathrm{d}t\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

(69)

Both terms on the right-hand-side can be bounded by the Holder’s property of
\nabla^{2}f_{k}
. Specifically, we have the following proposition:

Proposition B.3
.

Let
h:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}
,
\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\bm{\Sigma}\succ\bm{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{d\times d}
. Further define
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle g(\bm{x})=h(\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}+\bm{\mu}),\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

and use
f_{g}
to denote the solution to Stein’s equation

\displaystyle\Delta f(\bm{x})-\bm{x}^{\top}\nabla f(\bm{x})=g(\bm{x})-\mathbb{E}[g(\bm{z})]

where
\bm{z}
is the
d
-dimensional standard Gaussian distribution. It can then be guaranteed that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{x})-\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{y})\right\|\lesssim(2+\log(d\|\bm{\Sigma}\|))^{+}\cdot\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\|_{2}+e^{-1}.

(70)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.3
.
∎

Proposition
B.3
guarantees for every
t\in[0,1]
that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}f_{k}(t\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq(2+\log(d\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime}\|))^{+}\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}+e^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq(2+\log(d\|(n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}+\bm{\Sigma})\|))^{+}\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}+e^{-1}

(71)

Consequently, the first term on the right hand side of (
69
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{Tr}\left\{\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{k}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq(2+\log(d\|(n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}+\bm{\Sigma})\|))^{+}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]+e^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]

Here, we further notice that since
\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime}
is measurable with respect to
\mathscr{F}_{k-1}
, and that
\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}
are positively correlated
3
3

3

We refer readers to Appendix
D.4
for the details of this claim.
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right].

(72)

Therefore, the upper bound can be further simplified by

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{Tr}\left\{\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{k}\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\bm{P}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq(2+\log(d\|(n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}+\bm{\Sigma})\|))^{+}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right].

Meanwhile, the second term can also be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{1}(\bm{P}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})^{\top}(\nabla^{2}f_{k}(t\bm{P}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}+\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k})-\nabla^{2}f_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{z}}_{k}))(\bm{P}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k})\mathrm{d}t\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq(2+\log(d\|(n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}+\bm{\Sigma})\|))^{+}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right].

Since these two bounds are equivalent, in combination, the triangle inequality guarantees

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\left[h(\bm{S}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k-1}+\bm{T}_{k})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\log(d\|(n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}+\bm{\Sigma})\|))^{+}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right].

Plugging into (
64
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{T}_{1}^{\prime})]|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k}+\bm{T}_{k+1}^{\prime})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\bm{S}_{k-1}+\bm{T}_{k}^{\prime})]|

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\log(d\|(n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}+\bm{\Sigma})\|))^{+}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\bm{P}_{k}+\bm{\Sigma})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\bm{P}_{k}+\bm{\Sigma})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right].

(73)

We now aim to proof the last summation on the right-hand-side of (
73
). The law of total expectation directly implies, for every
k\in[n]
, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\bm{P}_{k}+\bm{\Sigma})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-1}\bm{V}_{k})\mid\mathscr{F}_{0}\right].

To further feature the summation, we invoke a telescoping technique. By taking
\bm{A}=\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime}
and
\bm{B}=\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime}
in Theorem
A.5
, the summand can be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-1}\bm{V}_{k})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-1}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime}-\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime}))\leq\mathsf{Tr}(\log(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime}))-\mathsf{Tr}(\log(\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime})).

Summing from
k=1
through
k=n
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\bm{P}_{k}+\bm{\Sigma})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime-1}\bm{V}_{k})\mid\mathscr{F}_{0}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Tr}(\log(\bm{P}_{k}^{\prime}))-\mathsf{Tr}(\log(\bm{P}_{k+1}^{\prime}))\big|\mathscr{F}_{0}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Tr}(\log\bm{P}_{1}^{\prime})]-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Tr}(\log\bm{P}_{n}^{\prime})]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}(\log(n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}+\bm{\Sigma}))-\log(\bm{\Sigma})).

(74)

Our target result follows by combining (
63
), (
73
) and (
74
).

B.3
Proof of Corollary
3.4

In order to simplify the upper bound in Theorem
3.3
, we firstly take
\bm{\Sigma}=\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
, yielding

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{W}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bm{x}_{k},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(2+\log(dn\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}\|))^{+}}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\bm{P}_{k}+\bm{\Sigma}_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\mathsf{Tr}(\log((n+1)\bm{\Sigma}_{n}))-\log(\bm{\Sigma_{n}}))\right]+\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma_{n}})}{n}}.

For the first term on the second line, we observe

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}(\log((n+1)\bm{\Sigma}_{n}))-\log(\bm{\Sigma_{n}}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\log(\lambda_{i}((n+1)\bm{\Sigma}_{n}))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\log(\lambda_{i}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\log(n+1)+\log\lambda_{i}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n}))-\log\lambda_{i}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})=d\log(n+1).

Meanwhile, the condition (
10
) can be invoked on the first term to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\bm{P}_{k}+\bm{\Sigma}_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq M\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\bm{P}_{k}+\bm{\Sigma}_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathscr{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=M\cdot\left[\mathsf{Tr}(\log((n+1)\bm{\Sigma}_{n}))-\log(\bm{\Sigma_{n}}))\right]

\displaystyle=Md\log(n+1),

where the third line follows from (
74
). In combination, the Wasserstein distance can be bounded by

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{W}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bm{x}_{k},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{M(2+\log(dn\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}\|))^{+}+1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot d\log n+\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma_{n}})}{n}}.

Comparison with the corresponding uni-dimensional Corollary 2.3 in
Röllin [2018]

When
d=1
, the condition (
10
) reduces to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|x_{k}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\leq M\mathbb{E}[x_{k}^{2}]\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\quad\forall k\in[n].

Notice that this is a weaker assumption than the one outlined in Equation (2.13) of
Röllin [2018]
, which further assumes that the conditional third momentum of
x_{k}
is uniformly bounded. In our proof of Corollary
3.4
, we invoke a telescoping method that not only simplifies the proof but also yields a tighter upper bound.

Comments on Corollary 3 in
Anastasiou et al. [2019]

In a similar attempt to generalize this uni-dimensional corollary from
Röllin [2018]
to multi-dimensional settings, Corollary 3 of
Anastasiou et al. [2019]
made the assumption that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{x}_{k}\|_{2}^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\leq\beta\vee\delta\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{V}_{k}),\quad\text{a.s.}

(75)

and applied the technique used by
Röllin [2018]
, which involved defining a sequence of stopping times
\{\tau_{k}\}_{k\in[n]}
. However, this generalization is non-trivial since the positive semi-definite order of symmetric matrices is
incomplete
; in other words, when
\bm{A},\bm{B}\in\mathbb{S}^{d\times d}
,
\bm{A}\npreceq\bm{B}
does not imply
\bm{A}\succ\bm{B}
. Consequently, the derivation from Equation (A.36) to (A.37) in the proof of Corollary 3 of
Anastasiou et al. [2019]
is invalid: apparently, the authors’ reasoning was, under their notation:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}(\overline{\bm{V}}_{\tau_{k}}-\overline{\bm{V}}_{\tau_{k-1}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Tr}(\overline{\bm{V}}_{\tau_{k}}-\overline{\bm{V}}_{\tau_{k-1}+1})+\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{V}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{Tr}\left(\frac{k}{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{n}-\frac{k-1}{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{n}\right)+\beta^{\frac{2}{3}}=\frac{1}{n}\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})+\beta^{\frac{2}{3}}

where the inequality on the second line follows from
\bm{V}_{\tau_{k}}\preceq\frac{k}{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
, and that
\bm{V}_{\tau_{k-1}+1}\npreceq\frac{k-1}{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
. However, since the positive semi-definite order is incomplete, the fact that
\bm{V}_{\tau_{k-1}+1}\npreceq\frac{k-1}{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
is not equivalent to
\bm{V}_{\tau_{k-1}+1}\succ\frac{k-1}{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
; neither is there any guarantee that
\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{V}_{\tau_{k-1}+1})
is greater than
\frac{k-1}{n}\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})
.

In our proof of Corollary
3.4
, we solve this problem by invoking a different assumption (
10
) than (
75
), and the telescoping method.

Comments on
Cattaneo et al. [2022]

Without assuming
\bm{P}_{1}=n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
almost surely,
Cattaneo et al. [2022]
established the following high-dimensional Berry–Esseen bound in convex distance:

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{c}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{x}_{i},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\inf_{\eta>0}\left\{\frac{(\beta_{2,2}d)^{\frac{1}{3}}}{\sqrt{n}\eta}+\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}\|\cdot d}{n\eta^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+\eta\sqrt{\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{-1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}}\right\},

(76)

where

\displaystyle\beta_{2,2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{3}+\|\bm{V}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}^{3}].

Here
\bm{z}_{1},\bm{z}_{2},\ldots,\bm{z}_{n}
are i.i.d. standard Gaussian random variables independent of
\mathscr{F}_{n}
.
The second term on the right-hand-side of (
76
) captures the discrepancy between
\bm{P}_{1}
and
n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
, while
the last term arises from the Gaussian anti-concentration inequality over the set of convex sets (see, e.g., Theorem
A.17
in the supplementary material, which we also apply in our proof of Theorem
3.2
.) However,
\beta_{2,2}
necessarily grows at least linearly in
n
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{V}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}\|\bm{V}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{V}_{i})]\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\geq n^{-1/2}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{V}_{i})]\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}=n[\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})]^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Consequently, the bound given by (
76
) cannot decay faster than

\displaystyle\inf_{\eta>0}\left\{\frac{(\beta_{2,2}d)^{\frac{1}{3}}}{\sqrt{n}\eta}+\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}\|\cdot d}{n\eta^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+\eta\sqrt{\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{-1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\eta>0}\left\{n^{-\frac{1}{6}}d^{\frac{1}{3}}[\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{-1}+\eta\sqrt{\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{-1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq d^{\frac{1}{6}}[\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})]^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{-1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{\frac{1}{4}}n^{-\frac{1}{12}},

where the last line invokes the AM-GM inequality. This yields a convergence rate of at best
O(n^{-1/12})
, which is slower than the
O(n^{-1/4}\log n)
rate obtained in Theorem
3.2
. The improvement in our result comes from exploiting the Markov chain structure to derive a sharper bound on the discrepancy between
\bm{P}_{1}
and
n\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
, together with a Stein’s equation argument that tightens the contribution of the first term in (
76
).

B.4
Proof of Corollary
3.1

Since
\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid s)}[\bm{f}_{i}(s,s^{\prime})]=\bm{0}
holds for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
i\in[n]
, it can be guaranteed that

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{n}\bm{f}_{i}(s_{i-1},s_{i})\right\}_{i=1}^{n}

is a martingale difference sequence adapted to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
; therefore, by defining

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{i}:=\frac{1}{n}\bm{f}_{i}(s_{i-1},s_{i}),

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{i}:=\sum_{j=1}^{i}\bm{X}_{j},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle W_{i}:=\sum_{j=1}^{i}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{X}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathscr{F}_{j-1},

we can apply Theorem
A.20
to obtain that for every
\sigma^{2}
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, it can be guaranteed with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2}
that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{f}_{i}(s_{i-1},s_{i})\right\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\log\frac{1}{\delta}}+\frac{F}{n}\log\frac{1}{\delta},\quad\text{or}\quad W_{n}\geq\sigma^{2}.

(77)

In what follows, we aim to bound the
W_{n}
by controlling its different from
\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})
. Specifically, we can define

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{i-1}(s)=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid s)}\|\bm{f}_{i}(s,s^{\prime})\|_{2}^{2}

and apply Corollary
A.22
with
a_{i}=0
,
b_{i}=F^{2}/n^{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle W_{n}-\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}[g_{i}(s_{i-1})-\mu(g_{i})]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{q}{1-\lambda}}\frac{F}{\sqrt{n}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right)

with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2}
. Hence, the triangle inequality directly yields

\displaystyle W_{n}\leq\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})+\sqrt{\frac{q}{1-\lambda}}\frac{F}{\sqrt{n}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right)

(78)

The theorem follows by combining (
77
), (
78
) using a union bound argument and taking

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}=\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})+\sqrt{\frac{q}{1-\lambda}}\frac{F}{\sqrt{n}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right).

B.5
Proof of Corollary
3.2

Part of the proof is inspired by the proof of Lemma B.8 in
Cattaneo et al. [2022]
and Theorem 2.1 in
Belloni and Oliveira [2018]
. We firstly notice that the convex distance satisfies

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{f}_{i}(s_{i-1},s_{i}),\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\right)=d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{f}_{i}(s_{i-1},s_{i}),\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I})\right);

hence, we can assume without loss of generality that
\bm{\Sigma}_{n}=\bm{I}
.
4
4

4

To see this, take
\bm{g}_{i}=\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{f}_{i}
and the proof follows for the sequence
\{\bm{g}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
.

Borrowing the notation from the proof of Theorem
3.3
and Corollary
3.4
, we define, for each
k=1,\ldots,n
,

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{k}:=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bm{f}_{j}(s_{j-1},s_{j}),

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{k}:=\mathbb{E}[\bm{f}_{k}(s_{k-1},s_{k})\bm{f}_{k}^{\top}(s_{k-1},s_{k})\mid\mathscr{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\bm{T}_{k}:=\sum_{j=k}^{n}\bm{V}_{j}^{1/2}\bm{z}_{j},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\bm{P}_{k}:=\sum_{j=k}^{n}\bm{V}_{j}.

This proof approaches the theorem in four steps:

1.

Find a value
\kappa=\kappa(n)=O(\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}})
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{I}\|\geq n\kappa)\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

2.

Construct a martingale
\{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
, whose differentiation satisfies the condition of Theorem
3.3
,
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}^{\top}]=n(1+\kappa)\bm{I},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{P}\left(\|\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\|_{2}>\sqrt{2d\kappa n\log n}\right)\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

3.

Apply Theorem
3.3
to
\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}
to derive a Berry–Esseen bound between the distributions of
\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}
and
\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},(1+\kappa)\bm{I}))
;

4.

Combine the results above to achieve the desired Berry–Esseen bound.

Step 1: find
\kappa

Due to the Markovian property, the matrix
\bm{V}_{k}
is a function of
s_{k-1}
for every
k\in[n]
. Define

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{V}}_{k}=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\mu,s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid s)}[\bm{f}_{i}(s,s^{\prime})\bm{f}_{i}^{\top}(s,s^{\prime})],

then it can be guaranteed that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{k-1}\sim\mu}[\bm{V}_{k}]=\overline{\bm{V}}_{k},

and that

\displaystyle\|\bm{V}_{k}-\overline{\bm{V}}_{k}\|\leq M_{k}^{2},\quad\text{a.s.}

hold for every
k\in[n]
.
Consequently, a direct application of Theorem
3.5
yields

\displaystyle\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{I}\|=n\cdot\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{V}_{k}-\overline{\bm{V}}_{k})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}^{4}}\sqrt{\frac{20q}{1-\lambda}\log\left(\frac{2d}{n^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}^{4}}\sqrt{\frac{40q}{1-\lambda}\log\left(2dn\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right)},

with probability at least
1-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. In what follows, we take

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}^{4}}{n}\frac{40q}{1-\lambda}\log\left(2dn\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right)}=\frac{\overline{M}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{40q}{1-\lambda}\log\left(2dn\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right)}.

Step 2: Construct
\{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}

Define the stopping time

\displaystyle\tau:=\sup\left\{t\leq n:\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{V}_{i}\preceq n(1+\kappa)\bm{I}\right\},

and let

\displaystyle m:=\left\lceil\frac{d}{M^{2}}\left\|n(1+\kappa)\bm{I}-\sum_{i=1}^{\tau}\bm{V}_{i}\right\|\right\rceil,\quad\text{and}\quad N:=\left\lceil\frac{dn(1+\kappa)}{M^{2}}\right\rceil+n.

By definition, it can be guaranteed that
n+m\leq N
, and that
N\asymp n
. We now construct a martingale difference process
\{\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
in the following way: for
1\leq i\leq\tau
, let
\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}=\bm{f}_{i}(s_{i-1},s_{i})
and for
\tau<i\leq\tau+m
, let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\left(n(1+\kappa)\bm{I}-\sum_{i=1}^{\tau}\bm{V}_{i}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{\epsilon}_{i},

where
\bm{\epsilon_{i}}=(\epsilon_{i1},\epsilon_{i2},\cdots,\epsilon_{id})
and
\{\epsilon_{ij}\}_{\tau<i\leq\tau+m,1\leq j\leq d}
are
i.i.d.
Rademacher random variables independent of the
s_{i}
’s, i.e.

\displaystyle\epsilon_{ij}=\begin{cases}+1,\quad\text{w.p. }\frac{1}{2};\\
-1,\quad\text{w.p. }\frac{1}{2}.\end{cases}

In particular, it holds that, for any
\tau<i\leq\tau+m
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}]=\bm{0},\quad\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}\mid\mathscr{F}_{i-1}]=\frac{1}{m}\left(n(1+\kappa)\bm{I}-\sum_{i=1}^{\tau}\bm{V}_{i}\right),

and

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{d}{m}\left\|n(1+\kappa)\bm{I}-\sum_{i=1}^{\tau}\bm{V}_{i}\right\|\leq M^{2}

almost surely. Finally, if
\tau+m<i\leq N
, we simply set
\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}=\bm{0}
.

The martingale
\{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
is naturally constructed by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{j}\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}.

In this step, we explore the difference between
\bm{S}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}
. Specifically, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\|_{2}>\sqrt{2d\kappa n\log n}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\|_{2}>\sqrt{2d\kappa n\log n},\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{I}\|\leq\kappa n)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\|_{2}>\sqrt{2d\kappa n\log n},\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{I}\|>\kappa n)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\|_{2}>\sqrt{2d\kappa n\log n},\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{I}\|\leq\kappa n)+\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{I}\|>\kappa n)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\|_{2}>\sqrt{2d\kappa n\log n},\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{I}\|\leq\kappa n)+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

To bound the first term on the left hand side of the last inequality, notice that, when
\|\bm{P}_{1}-n\bm{I}\|\leq\kappa n
,

\displaystyle\bm{P}_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{V}_{i}\preceq n(1+\kappa)\bm{I}.

Thus, on the same event,
\tau=n
and for every
j\in[d]
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\leq\|n(1+\kappa)\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{1}\|\leq 2\kappa n.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=n+1}^{n+m}\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\left\|(n(1+\kappa)\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=n+1}^{n+m}\bm{\epsilon}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|n(1+\kappa)\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{1}\|}{m}\left\|\sum_{i=n+1}^{n+m}\bm{\epsilon}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\kappa n}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{i=n+1}^{n+m}\epsilon_{ij}\right)^{2}.

Since
\{\epsilon_{ij}\}_{n+1\leq i\leq n+m,1\leq j\leq d}
are
i.i.d.
Rademacher random variables, the Hoeffding’s inequality guarantees that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=n+1}^{n+m}\epsilon_{ij}\right|\lesssim\sqrt{m\log n}

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. As a direct consequence, the difference between
\bm{S}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\|\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim 2d\kappa n\log n

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. In combination, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}\|_{2}\gtrsim\sqrt{2d\kappa n\log n})\leq 3n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 3: Berry–Esseen bound on
\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}

It can be easily verified that the sequence
\{\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
is a martingale difference such that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}\mid\mathscr{F}_{i-1}]=n(1+\kappa)\bm{I},\quad\text{and}\quad\|\bm{\widetilde{x}}_{i}\|_{2}\leq M\quad\text{a.s., }\forall i\in[N].

Hence, Corollary
3.4
can be applied on
\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}
to obtain

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{W}}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},(1+\kappa)\bm{I})\right)\lesssim Md\log d\cdot\frac{\log^{2}n}{\sqrt{n}}.

Step 4: Completing the proof

By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I})\right)\leq d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},(1+\kappa)\bm{I})\right)+d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}),\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},(1+\kappa)\bm{I})\right)

(79)

where the second term on the right-hand-side can be bounded using a direct application of Theorem
A.16
by

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}),\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},(1+\kappa)\bm{I})\right)\lesssim\kappa\|\bm{I}\|_{\mathsf{F}}\leq\kappa\sqrt{d}=O\left(\overline{M}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\log n\right).

(80)

For the first term, consider any convex set
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the triangle inequality guarantees, for every
x>0
, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A},\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}\leq x\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A},\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}>x\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}^{x}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}>x\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}^{x}\right)+d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}>x\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)+(1+\kappa)^{-1/2}d^{\frac{1}{4}}x+d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}>x\right).

(81)

Here,
\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},n(\bm{\Sigma}_{n}+\kappa\bm{I}))
and we applied Theorem
A.17
on the last inequality. On the right-most part of the inequality, the third term can be bounded by the Berry–Esseen bound on
\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}
and Theorem
A.18
:

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\|(1+\kappa)^{-1}\bm{I}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{d_{\mathsf{W}}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\kappa)^{-\frac{1}{4}}d^{\frac{1}{8}}\cdot\sqrt{M}d^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}dn^{-\frac{1}{4}}\log n

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{M}d^{\frac{5}{8}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}dn^{-\frac{1}{4}}\log n

Therefore, taking
x=\sqrt{2d\kappa\log n}
in (
81
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)+d^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot\sqrt{2d\kappa\log n}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{M}d^{\frac{5}{8}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}dn^{-\frac{1}{4}}\log n+3n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

A simple reorganization yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{\overline{M}\left(\frac{q}{1-\lambda}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}d^{\frac{3}{4}}\log^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(d\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right)+\sqrt{M}d^{\frac{5}{8}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}d\right\}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\log n.

(82)

Meanwhile, a union bound argument and the triangle inequality guarantee

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\cup\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}>x\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}>x\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}^{-x}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}>x\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}^{-x}\right)-d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}>x\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)-(1+\kappa)^{-\frac{1}{2}}d^{\frac{1}{4}}x-d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{S}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}>x\right);

consequently, it can be symmetrically proved that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\bm{T}}_{N}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{\overline{M}\left(\frac{q}{1-\lambda}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}d^{\frac{3}{4}}\log^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(d\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right)+\sqrt{M}d^{\frac{5}{8}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}d\right\}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\log n.

(83)

The theorem follows by combining (
79
), (
80
), (
82
), (
83
) and taking a supremum over
\mathcal{A}\in\mathscr{C}
.

Appendix C
Proof of results regarding TD learning

Throughout this section, we denote

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}=\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}^{\star},\quad\forall t\in[T],\quad\text{and}

(84)

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}=\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{T}-\bm{\theta}^{\star}.

(85)

Furthermore, for every
t=0,1,\ldots,T
, we denote

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\cdot]:=\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid s_{0},s_{1},\ldots,s_{t}].

Without any subscript, the operator
\mathbb{E}
represents taking expectation with respect to all the samples starting from
s_{0}
.

C.1
L^{2}
convergence of the TD estimation error

The following theorem captures the asymptotic property of
\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
with Markov samples and is useful in our proofs for other results.
Note that the bound holds, non-asymptotically, for all
t\geq t^{\star}
where
t^{\star}
is a problem-dependent quantity; we state it as an asymptotic result only for convenience.

Theorem C.1
.

Consider TD with Polyak-Ruppert averaging (
4.1
) with Markov samples and decaying stepsizes
\eta_{t}=\eta_{0}t^{-\alpha}
for
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
. Suppose that the Markov transition kernel has a unique stationary distribution, a strictly positive spectral gap, and mixes exponentially as indicated by Assumption
4.1
. It can then be guaranteed that when
t\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\big]\lesssim(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}\left[\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{2}}\frac{\eta_{0}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}t^{-\alpha}\log^{2}t+o\left(t^{-\alpha}\log^{2}t\right)\right].

Proof.

We firstly construct an iterative relation along the sequence
\{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\}_{t\geq 0}
in general, and then refine our analysis using a specific choice of stepsizes. The TD iteration rule (
4.1
) directly implies that

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}-2\eta_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{b}_{t})+\eta_{t}^{2}\|\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{b}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}-2\eta_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}+\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\bm{b}_{t})+2\eta_{t}^{2}(\|\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t}\|_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle=\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}-2\eta_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{A\Delta}_{t-1}-2\eta_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}

\displaystyle-2\eta_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})+2\eta_{t}^{2}(\|\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}).

Since
\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{A\Delta}_{t-1}\geq\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{t-1}
due to (
28
) and
\|\bm{A}_{t}\|\leq 1+\gamma
, we can bound
\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}-2\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)\eta_{t}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}+2\eta_{t}^{2}(1+\gamma)^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle-2\eta_{t}\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}]-2\eta_{t}\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]+2\eta_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{1}}{\underbrace{\left(1-2\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)\eta_{t}+2\eta_{t}^{2}(1+\gamma)^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}}}+\underset{I_{2}}{\underbrace{2\eta_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}}

\displaystyle-2\eta_{t}\underset{I_{3}}{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}]}}-2\eta_{t}\underset{I_{4}}{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]}}.

(86)

In this expression,
I_{1}
is contractive with respect to
\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}
as long as
\eta_{t}
is sufficiently small, while
I_{2}
is proportional to
\eta_{t}^{2}
since
\mathbb{E}\|\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
is independent of
t
. These two terms are desirable and can be left as they are;

The difficulty of this proof lies in bounding
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
using Markov samples. Notice that with
i.i.d.
sampling, both terms are actually
0
; hence, we aim to bound them by applying the mixing property of the Markov chain.

To simplify notation, throughout the proof, we denote

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{mix}}:=t_{\mathsf{mix}}(\eta_{t})+1,

so that with Markov samples,
s_{t-1}\mid\mathscr{F}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sim P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}-1}(\cdot\mid s_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})
, and that

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{TV}}(P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}-1}(\cdot\mid s_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}),\mu)\leq\eta_{t}.

Meanwhile, Assumption
4.1
implies that

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{mix}}\leq\frac{\log(m/\eta_{t})}{\log(1/\rho)}+1=\frac{\log(m/\eta_{0})+\alpha\log t}{\log(1/\rho)}+1<\frac{\log(m/\eta_{0})+\alpha\log t}{1-\rho}+1.

(87)

In other words,
t_{\mathsf{mix}}(\eta_{t})
grows at most by
O(\log t)
; therefore, in what follows, we can assume that
t
is large enough such that
t\geq 2t_{\mathsf{mix}}
. The essential idea of bounding
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
involves decomposing
\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}
by

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}=(\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})+\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}},

where the norm of
(\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})
is bounded by the decaying stepsizes, while the correlation between
\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}
and
\bm{A}_{t},\bm{b}_{t}
is bounded by the mixing property of the Markov chain.

We address
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
respectively.

Bounding
I_{3}

The definition of
t_{\mathsf{mix}}
implies

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}]]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}_{s_{t-1}\sim P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}-1}(\cdot\mid s_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})}[\mathbb{E}_{s_{t}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{t-1})}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}]]-\mathbb{E}_{s_{t-1}\sim\mu}[\mathbb{E}_{s_{t}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{t-1})}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}]\right]]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\sup_{s_{t-1}}|\mathbb{E}_{s_{t}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{t-1})}\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}|\cdot d_{\mathsf{TV}}(P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}-1}(\cdot\mid s_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}),\mu)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[2\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}]\cdot\eta_{t};

notice that the inequality on the fourth line follows from the basic property of the TV distance.
As a direct consequence,
I_{3}
is featured by

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}]+2\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}-\bm{A})(\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}-\bm{A})(\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})]

\displaystyle\geq-2\eta_{t}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}-4\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}]-2\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-2\eta_{t}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}-4\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}\|\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}]-2\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}.

To lower bound the right-hand-side of the last expression, the following lemma comes in handy, with its proof postponed to Appendix
D.5
.

Lemma C.2
.

For the TD iterations (
4.1
) with Markov samples and non-increasing stepsizes
\eta_{1}\geq\ldots\geq\eta_{T}
it holds that, for all
t\geq t_{\mathsf{mix}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}\leq t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta^{\star}}\|_{2}+1)+2\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2};

(88a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}\left[t_{\mathsf{mix}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}+4\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right];

(88b)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}\|\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}]

\displaystyle\leq t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}+\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}.

(88c)

Lemma
C.2
directly leads to

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle\geq-2\eta_{t}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}-4t_{\mathsf{mix}}^{2}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}(2\|\bm{\theta^{\star}}\|_{2}+1)^{2}-16t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle-4t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}-4\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-4t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-(2\eta_{t}+4t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}-(16t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}+4\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle-4t_{\mathsf{mix}}^{2}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}(2\|\bm{\theta^{\star}}\|_{2}+1)^{2}-4t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}.

(89)

Bounding
I_{4}

Similarly, we decompose
I_{4}
as

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]+\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]+\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})].

The first term can be bounded using the
t_{\mathsf{mix}}
separation:

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{s_{t-1}\sim P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}-1}(\cdot\mid s_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})}[\mathbb{E}_{s_{t}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{t-1})}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]]-\mathbb{E}_{s_{t-1}\sim\mu}[\mathbb{E}_{s_{t}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{t-1})}[\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]]]|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\sup_{s_{t-1}}|\mathbb{E}_{s_{t}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{t-1})}\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})|\cdot d_{\mathsf{TV}}(P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}-1}(\cdot\mid s_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}),\mu)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\cdot\eta_{t},

while the second term can be bounded by stepsizes:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{t})]

\displaystyle\geq-(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq-(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left[t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)+2\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=-\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left[t_{\mathsf{mix}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)+2\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}\right].

Therefore,
I_{4}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\geq-\eta_{t}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle-\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left[t_{\mathsf{mix}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)+2\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}\right].

(90)

Combining terms

With
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
bounded, we now return to Equation (
86
).
\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq I_{1}+I_{2}-2\eta_{t}(I_{3}+I_{4})

\displaystyle\leq[1-2\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)\eta_{t}+2\eta_{t}^{2}(1+\gamma)^{2}]\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}+2\eta_{t}^{2}(2\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}+1)^{2}

\displaystyle+2\eta_{t}(2\eta_{t}+4t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}+2\eta_{t}(16t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}+4\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}})\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+8\eta_{t}t_{\mathsf{mix}}^{2}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}(2\|\bm{\theta^{\star}}\|_{2}+1)^{2}+8\eta_{t}t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+2\eta_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)+2\eta_{t}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle+4\eta_{t}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}.

(91)

Specifying the polynomially-decaying stepsizes

With polynomially-decaying stepsizes, when
t
is sufficiently large, it can be guaranteed that
t>2t_{\mathsf{mix}}
, and therefore
\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\geq 2^{-\alpha}\eta_{t}
. Furthermore, for sufficiently large
t
,
\eta_{t}(1+\gamma)^{2}<\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)
. Therefore, by dividing
(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}
on both sides and combining terms, we can simplify Equation (
91
) as

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\widetilde{C}_{1}t^{-\alpha})\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}}{(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}}+\widetilde{C}_{2}t^{-2\alpha}\log^{2}t+\widetilde{C}_{3}t^{-2\alpha}\log t\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}}{{(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}}}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}_{4}t^{-2\alpha}\log t\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}{{(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}}}+\widetilde{C}_{5}t^{-2\alpha}\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-2}\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}}{2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1},

(92)

where
\widetilde{C}_{1}
through
\widetilde{C}_{5}
are constants depending on
\alpha,\eta_{0},m
and
\rho
. Notice that the
\log t
terms occur due to
t_{\mathsf{mix}}=O(\log t)
; see (
87
). We will use an induction argument based on the relation (
92
). For simplicity, let

\displaystyle X_{t}=\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}}{2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1};

now suppose that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{2}]\leq\widetilde{C}\cdot\frac{\log^{2}t}{t^{\alpha}},\quad\forall 1<t\leq k,

(93)

for some
\widetilde{C}
.

Our goal is to demonstrate, inductively, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{k+1}^{2}]\leq\widetilde{C}\cdot\frac{\log^{2}(k+1)}{(k+1)^{\alpha}}.

(94)

Towards this end, the iterative relation (
92
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{k+1}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\widetilde{C}_{1}(k+1)^{-\alpha}\right)\mathbb{E}[X_{k}^{2}]+\widetilde{C}_{2}(k+1)^{-2\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-2\alpha}\log(k+1)\mathbb{E}[X_{k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}_{4}(k+1)^{-2\alpha}\log(k+1)\sum_{i=k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}_{5}(k+1)^{-2\alpha}\sum_{i=k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}].

(95)

Here, the induction assumption guarantees that, as long as
k>2t_{\mathsf{mix}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{k}^{2}]\leq\widetilde{C}k^{-\alpha}\log^{2}k,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}]\leq\widetilde{C}(k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}})^{-\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}})<2^{-\alpha}\widetilde{C}(k+1)^{-\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1),

and that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]

\displaystyle\leq t_{\mathsf{mix}}\cdot\widetilde{C}(k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}})^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\log(k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{C}\cdot(k+1)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\log^{2}(k+1),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq t_{\mathsf{mix}}\cdot\widetilde{C}(k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}})^{-\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1-t_{\mathsf{mix}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{C}\cdot(k+1)^{-\alpha}\log^{3}(k+1).

Plugging these inequalities into the iteration relation (
95
), we obtain that for sufficiently large
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{k+1}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}\cdot\left[k^{-\alpha}\log^{2}k-\widetilde{C}_{1}(k+1)^{-\alpha}k^{-\alpha}\log^{2}k+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{5}{2}\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)\right]

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}_{2}(k+1)^{-2\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1).

Here,
\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2}
and
\widetilde{C}_{3}
are again constants independent of
t
, with there exact values can change from (
95
). Therefore, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}\cdot\left[k^{-\alpha}\log^{2}k-\widetilde{C}_{1}(k+1)^{-\alpha}k^{-\alpha}\log^{2}k+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{5}{2}\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)\right]

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}_{2}(k+1)^{-2\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)\leq\widetilde{C}(k+1)^{-\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1).

(96)

Notice that when
x
is sufficiently large, the function
f(x)=x^{-\alpha}\log^{2}(x)
is monotonically decreasing; therefore, for sufficiently large
k
, it can be guaranteed that
k^{-\alpha}\log^{2}k>(k+1)^{-\alpha}\log(k+1)
. Therefore, the left-hand-side of (
96
) is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}\cdot\left[k^{-\alpha}\log^{2}k-\widetilde{C}_{1}(k+1)^{-\alpha}k^{-\alpha}\log^{2}k+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{5}{2}\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)\right]

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}_{2}(k+1)^{-2\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}\cdot\left[k^{-\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)-\widetilde{C}_{1}(k+1)^{-\alpha}(k+1)^{-\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{5}{2}\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)\right]

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}_{2}(k+1)^{-2\alpha}\log^{2}(k+1)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{C}\log^{2}(k+1)\cdot\left[k^{-\alpha}+\left(\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}}{\widetilde{C}}-\widetilde{C}_{1}\right)(k+1)^{-2\alpha}+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{5}{2}\alpha}\right].

Hence, in order to prove (
96
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle k^{-\alpha}+\left(\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}}{\widetilde{C}}-\widetilde{C}_{1}\right)(k+1)^{-2\alpha}+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{5}{2}\alpha}\leq(k+1)^{-\alpha},

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle(k+1)^{2\alpha}\left[k^{-\alpha}-(k+1)^{-\alpha}\right]+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\leq\widetilde{C}_{1}-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}}{\widetilde{C}}.

Here, we further notice that the function
f(x)=x^{-\alpha}
is monotonically decreasing and convex, so
k^{-\alpha}-(k+1)^{-\alpha}=f(k)-f(k+1)\leq-f^{\prime}(k+1)=\alpha(k+1)^{-\alpha-1}
. Hence, the proof boils down to showing

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{1}-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}}{\widetilde{C}}

\displaystyle\geq(k+1)^{2\alpha}\cdot\alpha(k+1)^{-\alpha-1}+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}

\displaystyle=\alpha(k+1)^{\alpha-1}+\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}

for an appropriate
\widetilde{C}
that is independent of
t
and satisfies the induction assumption (
93
).
Towards this end, we define a function
f(\widetilde{C},k)
as

\displaystyle f(\widetilde{C},k):=\widetilde{C}_{1}-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}}{\widetilde{C}}-\alpha(k+1)^{\alpha-1}-\widetilde{C}_{3}(k+1)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}

It is easy to verify that for any
\widetilde{C}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}f(\widetilde{C},k)=\widetilde{C}_{1}-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}}{\widetilde{C}}.

Therefore, we can take

\displaystyle k^{\star}=\min\left\{k:f\left(\max_{1\leq t\leq k}\frac{t^{\alpha}}{\log^{2}t}\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{2}],k\right)\geq 0\right\},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}=\max_{1\leq t\leq k^{\star}}\frac{t^{\alpha}}{\log^{2}t}\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{2}].

On one hand, if
k^{\star}
does not exist, then from our analysis we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{2}]\leq\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}}{\widetilde{C}_{1}}\frac{\log^{2}t}{t^{\alpha}}

for all
t\geq 1
; on the other hand, if
k^{\star}
does exist, then an induction argument guarantees that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{2}]\leq\widetilde{C}\frac{\log^{2}t}{t^{\alpha}}

for all
t\geq 1
. In both cases, (
94
) holds true.

Specification of the coefficient

We next try to specify the coefficient corresponding to the leading term of the upper bound. In the previous paragraph, we have essentially proved that, there exists a
t^{\star}\in\mathbb{N}
depending on
\alpha,\eta_{0},\lambda_{0},m
and
\rho
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{2}]\leq 1,\quad\text{for all }\quad t\geq t^{\star}.

Hence, when
t>t^{\star}
, a closer examination of (
91
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{2}]\leq(1-\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)\eta_{t})\mathbb{E}[X_{t-1}^{2}]+C\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{2}}t^{-2\alpha}\log^{2}t

for a
universal constant

C
. Hence by iteration, it can be guaranteed that when
t>t^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{2}]\leq\underset{I_{1}}{\underbrace{\prod_{i=t^{\star}+1}^{t}(1-\beta i^{-\alpha})X_{t^{\star}}}}+C\underset{I_{2}}{\underbrace{\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{2}}\sum_{i=t^{\star}}^{t}(i^{-2\alpha}\log^{2}i)\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}(1-\beta k^{-\alpha})}}.

Here, it is easy to verify that
I_{1}
converges exponentially with respect to
t
, and that
I_{2}
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{2}}\log^{2}t\sum_{i=t^{\star}}^{t}i^{-2\alpha}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}(1-\beta k^{-\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{2}}\log^{2}t\sum_{i=1}^{t}i^{-2\alpha}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}(1-\beta k^{-\alpha})

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{2}}\log^{2}t\left(\frac{1}{\beta}t^{-\alpha}+O(t^{-1})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{0}}{(1-\rho)^{2}\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}t^{-\alpha}\log^{2}t+o(t^{-\alpha}\log^{2}t).

The theorem follows immediately.

∎

C.2
High-probability convergence guarantee for the original TD estimation error

Similar to the case with
i.i.d.
samples, we firstly state the following theorem for the high-probability convergence rate for the original TD estimation error
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
with Markov samples.

Theorem C.3
.

Consider TD with Polyak-Ruppert averaging (
4.1
) with Markov samples and decaying stepsizes
\eta_{t}=\eta_{0}t^{-\alpha}
for
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
. Suppose that the Markov transition kernel has a unique stationary distribution
\mu
, a strictly positive spectral gap
1-\lambda>0
, and Assumption
4.1
holds true. Then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists
\eta_{0}>0
, such that with probability at least
1-{\delta}
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{13}{2}\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+\frac{5}{4}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta_{0}\sqrt{\frac{2t_{\mathsf{mix}}(t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})}{(2\alpha-1)}\log\frac{9Tt_{\mathsf{mix}}(t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})}{\delta}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left(\frac{(1-\gamma)\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}}{4\alpha}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2(1-\alpha)}}t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}

hold simultaneously for all
t\in[T]
.

Proof.

Recalling the TD update rule (
4.1
), we represent
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
as

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}=\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}^{\star}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\eta_{t}(\bm{A}_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{b}_{t}))-\bm{\theta}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\eta_{t}(\bm{A}\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-\bm{b}+\bm{\zeta}_{t})

\displaystyle=(\bm{I}-\eta_{t}\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}-\eta_{t}\bm{\zeta}_{t},

where
\bm{\zeta}_{t}
is defined as

\displaystyle\bm{\zeta}_{t}:=(\bm{A}_{t}-\bm{A})\bm{\theta}_{t-1}-(\bm{b}_{t}-\bm{b}).

(97)

Therefore by induction,
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
can be expressed as a weighted sum of
\{\bm{\zeta}_{i}\}_{0\leq i<t}
, namely

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}=\prod_{k=1}^{t}(\bm{I}-\eta_{k}\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{0}-\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\bm{\zeta}_{i},

(98)

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad\bm{R}_{i}^{t}=\eta_{i}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t-1}(\bm{I}-\eta_{k}\bm{A}).

(99)

The difficulty in bounding the second term of (
98
) lies in the fact that with Markov samples,
\{\bm{\zeta_{i}}\}_{i>0}
is no longer a martingale difference process. Therefore, we further decompose
\bm{\zeta}_{i}
into three parts, namely

\displaystyle\bm{\zeta}_{i}=\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}]+(\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}])+(\bm{\zeta}_{i}-\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}).

(100)

In order to simplify notation, throughout this proof we denote

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{mix}}=t_{\mathsf{mix}}(\varepsilon)+1,

where
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
is to be specified later.
Furthermore, for every
t>0
, we denote

\displaystyle i_{\mathsf{mix}}=\max\{0,i-t_{\mathsf{mix}}(\varepsilon)\},\quad\text{and}

(101)

\displaystyle\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}=(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\theta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}-(\bm{b}_{i}-\bm{b}),

(102)

The intuition behind the construction of
\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}
is to guarantee that the samples
(\bm{A}_{i},\bm{b}_{i})
and the iterated estimator
\bm{\theta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}
are separated in the Markov chain by at least
t_{\mathsf{mix}}
