samples, so that their distributions are close to independent. Recall from (
132
) that the mixing property of the Markov chain featured by Assumption
4.1
guarantees

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{mix}}\leq\frac{\log(m/\varepsilon)}{\log(1/\rho)}+1.

(103)

Furthermore, the difference bewteen
\bm{\zeta}_{i}
and
\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}
is

\displaystyle\bm{\zeta}_{i}-\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}=(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\theta}_{i-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}).

(104)

Therefore, with the decomposition (
100
),
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
can be characterized as

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{1}}{\underbrace{\prod_{k=1}^{t}(\bm{I}-\eta_{k}\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{0}}}-\underset{I_{2}}{\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}]}}-\underset{I_{3}}{\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\theta}_{i-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}})}}

\displaystyle-\underset{I_{4}}{\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}])}}.

(105)

In what follows, we denote

\displaystyle\beta=\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\lambda_{0}\eta_{0},

\displaystyle R=\frac{13}{2}\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+\frac{5}{4},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{t}=\left\{\max_{1\leq i\leq t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}\leq R\right\}.

Furthermore, for any given
\delta
, let
5
5

5

Notice that the existence of
t^{\star}(\delta)
is guaranteed by a similar reasoning to that in the proof of Theorem B.1 in
Wu et al. [2024]
.

\displaystyle t^{\star}=t^{\star}(\delta):=\inf\Bigg\{t\in\mathbb{N}^{+}:

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{2\alpha-1}{2^{11}\eta_{0}^{2}}\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\log(8m\eta_{0}/\beta)}\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}x^{-\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{27}\cdot\frac{\log(8m\eta_{0}/\beta)}{\log(1/\rho)}\Bigg\},

(106)

and assume that

\displaystyle\eta_{0}\sqrt{\frac{2}{2\alpha-1}\frac{\log(8m\eta_{0}/\beta)}{\log(1/\rho)}}\max\left\{\frac{1}{4\sqrt{1-\alpha}},\log\frac{9\log(8m\eta_{0}/\beta)t^{\star}}{\log(1/\rho)\delta}\right\}\leq\frac{1}{32}.

(107)

We break down the proof of the theorem into a sequence of steps:

1.

We obtain convergence rates for the four terms on the right-hand-side of (
105
);

2.

We lower bound the probability of
\mathcal{H}_{t^{\star}}
by
1-\frac{\delta}{3}
;

3.

We lower bound the probability of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
by
1-\frac{2\delta}{3}
;

4.

Using the results from the first steps, we arrive at a final bound on
\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}
.

Step 1: Basic convergence properties of the four terms on the right-hand-side of (
105
)

As is shown in the proof of Theorem B.1 in
Wu et al. [2024]
, the norm of
I_{1}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{k=0}^{t-1}(\bm{I}-\eta_{k}\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{0}\right\|\leq\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}t^{-\alpha}\|\bm{\Delta}_{0}\|_{2}.

(108)

For the term
I_{2}
, we observe that for
i\leq t_{\mathsf{mix}}
, since
i_{\mathsf{mix}}=0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\left[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{0}[(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\theta}_{0}-(\bm{b}_{i}-\bm{b})]=\bm{0};

otherwise when
i>t_{\mathsf{mix}}
, since
i_{\mathsf{mix}}=i-t_{\mathsf{mix}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[\bm{A}_{i}])\bm{\theta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{b}_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[\bm{b}_{i}]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq d_{\text{TV}}(P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(\cdot|s_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}),\mu)\left(\sup_{s_{i-1},s_{i}\in\mathcal{S}}\|\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}+\sup_{s_{i}\in\mathcal{S}}\|\bm{b}_{i}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)d_{\text{TV}}(P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(\cdot|s_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}),\mu)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1).

(109)

Meanwhile, we observe that the sum of
\|\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\|
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\|\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\eta_{i}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}\|\bm{I}-\eta_{k}\bm{A}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\eta_{0}i^{-\alpha}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}\left(1-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}k^{-\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\eta_{0}i^{-\alpha}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}(1-\beta k^{-\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{0}}{\beta}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left(\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}-\prod_{k=i}^{t}\right)(1-\beta k^{-\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{0}}{\beta}\left(1-\prod_{k=1}^{t}(1-\beta k^{-\alpha})\right)<\frac{\eta_{0}}{\beta},

where the second inequality follows from (
32
) in Lemma
A.6
.
Therefore, the norm of
I_{2}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left\|\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\right\|\left\|\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon\eta_{0}}{\beta}(2\max_{1\leq i\leq t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1).

(110)

For the term
I_{3}
, we firstly bound the difference betweeen
\bm{\theta}_{i}
and
\bm{\theta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}
by
R
and the initial stepsize
\eta_{0}
. Notice that given the induction assumption,

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\theta}_{i-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}(\bm{\theta}_{j}-\bm{\theta}_{j-1})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\eta_{j}(\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}_{j-1}-\bm{b}_{j})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{i-1}\eta_{j}\|\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}_{j-1}-\bm{b}_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{1-\alpha}\eta_{i}(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1).

Hence, the norm of
I_{3}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\theta}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\|\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\|\|\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A}\|\left\|\bm{\theta}_{i-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left(\eta_{i}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}\left(1-\beta k^{-\alpha}\right)\right)\cdot 4\cdot\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{1-\alpha}\eta_{i}(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{t}\eta_{i}^{2}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}\left(1-\beta k^{-\alpha}\right)\cdot\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{1-\alpha}(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{(1-\alpha)}(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{t}i^{-2\alpha}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}(1-\beta k^{-\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{1-\alpha}(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\cdot\frac{1}{2\alpha-1}\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}t^{-\alpha}.

(111)

Notice that the last inequality follows by taking
\nu=2\alpha
in (
36
) in Lemma
A.8
.

For the term
I_{4}
, we again invoke the vector Azuma’s inequality (Theorem
A.19
). For simplicity, we define

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{i}:=\bm{\zeta}_{i,\text{mix}}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\text{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\text{mix}}].

(112)

By definition, we can see that for every integer
r\in[t_{\mathsf{mix}}]
, the sequence
\{\bm{X}_{r+it_{\text{mix}}}\}_{i=0,1,\ldots}

form a martingale difference process. Throughout this part, we assume, without loss of generality that
t=t^{\prime}\cdot t_{\mathsf{mix}}
for a positive integer
t^{\prime}
. Hence, we can first consider the norm of the summation

\displaystyle\sum_{i^{\prime}=0}^{t^{\prime}}\bm{R}_{r+i^{\prime}t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t}\bm{X}_{r+i^{\prime}t_{\mathsf{mix}}},

(113)

which we will bound using vector Azuma’s inequality. Towards that goal, we define

\displaystyle W_{\max}^{r}:=\sum_{i^{\prime}=0}^{t^{\prime}}\sup\left\|\bm{R}_{r+i^{\prime}t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t}\bm{X}_{r+i^{\prime}t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

By definition, this term is bounded by

\displaystyle W_{\max}^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i^{\prime}=0}^{t^{\prime}}\|\bm{R}_{r+i^{\prime}t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t}\|^{2}\sup\|\bm{X}_{r+i^{\prime}t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sum_{i^{\prime}=0}^{t^{\prime}}\|\bm{R}_{r+i^{\prime}t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t}\|^{2}\cdot\max_{1\leq i\leq t}\sup\|\bm{X}_{i}\|_{2}^{2};

by summing over
r=1
through
r=t_{\mathsf{mix}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}W_{\max}^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=1}^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=0}^{t^{\prime}}\|\bm{R}_{r+i^{\prime}t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t}\|^{2}\cdot\max_{1\leq i\leq t}\sup\|\bm{X}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq t}\sup\|\bm{X}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{t}\|\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\|^{2}.

Meanwhile, the triangle inequality directly implies

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq t}\sup\|\bm{X}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq i\leq t}\sup\|\bm{\zeta}_{i,\text{mix}}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\text{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\text{mix}}]\|

\displaystyle\leq 4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1.

In combination, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sqrt{W_{\max}^{r}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sqrt{\sum_{r=1}^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}W_{\max}^{r}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sup\|\bm{X}_{i}\|_{2}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{t}\|\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{t}\|\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{t}\eta_{i}^{2}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}(1-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\lambda_{0}\eta_{k})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\cdot\eta_{0}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{t}i^{-2\alpha}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}(1-\beta k^{-\alpha})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\cdot\eta_{0}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\alpha-1}}\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2(1-\alpha)}}t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}},

where we invoked Lemma
A.8
in the last inequality, following the same logic as in the last line of (
111
). Consequently, the vector Azuma’s inequality (Theorem
A.19
) , combined with a union bound argument, yields the bound on the norm of
I_{4}

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}])\right\|_{2}\leq 2\sqrt{2\log\frac{9t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\delta_{t}}}\sum_{r=1}^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sqrt{W_{\max}^{r}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{0}\sqrt{\frac{2t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{2\alpha-1}\log\frac{3t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\delta_{t}}}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\cdot\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2(1-\alpha)}}t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}},

(114)

with probability at least
1-\delta_{t}/3
.

Step 2: Bounding
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t^{\star}})

By definition,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{0})=1
. We will show via induction that, for all other
1\leq t\leq t^{\star}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t})\geq 1-\frac{t}{3t^{\star}}\delta.

By taking
\varepsilon=\frac{\beta}{8\eta_{0}}
in (
110
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}]\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}\left(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1\right).

Next, putting together (
111
), condition (
107
) and the bound on
t_{\mathsf{mix}}
as specified by (
24
) guarantees that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\theta}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}\left(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1\right).

Similarly, setting
\delta_{t}=\frac{\delta}{3t^{\star}}
in (
114
) and using the condition (
107
), we have that, with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{3t^{\star}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}])\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{0}\sqrt{2\log\frac{9t_{\mathsf{mix}}t^{\star}}{\delta}}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1).

Therefore, when
\mathcal{H}_{t-1}
holds true, the norm of
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
can be bounded using the triangle inequality as

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left(2R+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1\right)+\frac{1}{8}\left(2R+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1\right)+\frac{1}{16}(4R+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4}R+\frac{13}{8}\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+\frac{5}{16}=R,

(115)

with probability of at least
1-\frac{\delta}{3t^{\star}}
. It then follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t-1}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{t})\leq\frac{\delta}{3t^{\star}},

and thus that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t^{\star}})\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{3}.

Step 3: Bounding
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{\infty})

For
t>t^{\star}
, we sharpen the induction argument by a more refined choice of
\delta_{t}
. In detail, let

\displaystyle\delta_{t}=3t_{\mathsf{mix}}(\beta/8\eta_{0})\exp\left\{-\frac{2\alpha-1}{2^{11}\eta_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{t_{\mathsf{mix}}(\beta/8\eta_{0})}\right)\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}t^{-\alpha}\right\}.

Then the norm of
I_{4}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}])\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{0}\sqrt{\frac{2t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{2\alpha-1}\log\frac{3t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\delta_{t}}}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\cdot\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2(1-\alpha)}}t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1).

with probability at least
1-\delta_{t}
. Hence, using induction, when
\mathcal{H}_{t-1}
holds true, the bound in (
115
) implies that
\mathcal{H}_{t}
also holds true with probability at least
1-\delta_{t}
. In other words,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t-1})-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t})=\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t-1}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{t})\leq\delta_{t}.

Consequently, the definition of
t^{\star}
(
106
) guarantees

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{\infty})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t^{\star}})-\sum_{t=t^{\star}+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t-1})-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{t})

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\delta}{3}\right)-\sum_{t=t^{\star}+1}^{\infty}\delta_{t}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\delta}{3}\right)-\int_{t^{\star}}^{\infty}3t_{\mathsf{mix}}(\beta/8\eta_{0})\exp\left\{-\frac{2\alpha-1}{2^{11}\eta_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{t_{\mathsf{mix}}(\beta/8\eta_{0})}\right)\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}x^{-\alpha}\right\}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\delta}{3}\right)-\frac{\delta}{3}=1-\frac{2\delta}{3}.

Step 4: Refining the bound on
\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{2}

In order to bound the norm of
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
by
O(t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})
and thus conclude the proof, we take
\varepsilon=t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}
. Then,

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{mix}}(\varepsilon)\leq\frac{\log m+\frac{\alpha}{2}\log t}{\log(1/\rho)}.

With this bound, (
110
), (
111
) and (
114
) yield that, with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{3T}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}]\right\|\leq\frac{\eta_{0}}{\beta}(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\theta}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}}{(1-\alpha)(2\alpha-1)}\cdot t_{\mathsf{mix}}(2\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}t^{-\alpha},

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{\zeta}_{i,\mathsf{mix}}])\right\|_{2}\leq 2\eta_{0}\sqrt{\frac{2t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{(2\alpha-1)}\log\frac{9Tt_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\delta}}(4\max_{1\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2(1-\alpha)}}t^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}.

The final result follows from the triangle inequality and the union bound.
∎

C.3
Proof of Theorem
4.2

Recall from Theorem
A.13
that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}-\bm{\Lambda}^{\star})=T^{\alpha-1}\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{X}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))+O(T^{2\alpha-2}),

where
\bm{X}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star})
is the solution to the Lyapunov equation

\displaystyle\eta_{0}(\bm{AX+XA}^{\top})=\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}.

By combining Lemma
A.6
, Lemma
A.7
and Lemma
A.12
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}(\bm{X}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathsf{Tr}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star})}{\eta_{0}\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\leq\frac{\|\bm{A}^{-1}\|^{2}\mathsf{Tr}(\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}})}{\eta_{0}\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\leq\frac{\mathsf{Tr}(\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}})}{\eta_{0}\lambda_{0}^{3}(1-\gamma)^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{m}{1-\rho}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{0}\lambda_{0}^{5}(1-\gamma)^{5}}.

Hence, the difference between
\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}
and
\bm{\Lambda}^{\star}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}-\bm{\Lambda}^{\star})\leq\widetilde{C}T^{\alpha-1},

where
\widetilde{C}
can be represented by
\lambda_{0},\eta_{0}
and
\gamma
.
Therefore, it suffices to show that with probability at least
1-\delta
, the averaged TD error can be bounded by

\displaystyle\|\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2\sqrt{\frac{2\mathsf{Tr}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T})}{T}\log\frac{6d}{\delta}}+o\left(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{dT}{\delta}\right).

As a direct implication of (
98
),
\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}
can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\prod_{k=1}^{t}(\bm{I}-\eta_{k}\bm{A})\Delta_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})-\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\prod_{k=1}^{t}(\bm{I}-\eta_{k}\bm{A})\Delta_{0}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\prod_{k=1}^{t}(\bm{I}-\eta_{k}\bm{A})\Delta_{0}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\sum_{t=i}^{T}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\sum_{t=i}^{T}\bm{R}_{i}^{t}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i-1},

where we have switched the order of summation in the last equation. The definition of
\bm{Q}_{t}
(
41
) implies

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}=\underset{I_{1}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{T\eta_{0}}\bm{Q}_{0}\bm{\Delta}_{0}}}-\underset{I_{2}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})}}-\underset{I_{3}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}}},

where
I_{1}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{T\eta_{0}}\bm{Q}_{0}\bm{\Delta}_{0}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{T\eta_{0}}\|\bm{Q}_{0}\|\|\bm{\Delta}_{0}\|\leq\frac{3}{T}\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\|\bm{\Delta}_{0}\|_{2}.

We now proceed to bounding
I_{2}
and
I_{3}
respectively.

Throughout the proof, we let

\displaystyle\beta=\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\lambda_{0}\eta_{0},

\displaystyle R=\frac{13}{2}\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+\frac{5}{4},

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{mix}}=t_{\mathsf{mix}}(T^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})\leq\frac{\log m+(\alpha/2)\log T}{\log(1/\rho)}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle R^{\prime}=\eta_{0}\sqrt{\frac{2t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{2\alpha-1}\log\frac{27Tt_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\delta}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left(\frac{\beta}{2\alpha}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2(1-\alpha)}},

and, for each
1\leq t\leq T
,

\mathcal{H}_{t}=\Big\{\|\bm{\Delta}_{j}\|_{2}\leq\min\{R^{\prime}j^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}},R\},\forall j\leq t\Big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}_{t}=\bm{\Delta}_{t}\mathds{1}(\mathcal{H}_{t}).

Theorem
C.3
shows that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{H}_{T})\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{3}
.

Bounding
I_{2}

In order to invoke the matrix Freedman’s inequality on the term
I_{2}
, we firstly relate it to a martingale. Specifically, for every
i\in[T]
, we define
\bm{U}_{i}
as

\displaystyle\bm{U}_{i}=\mathbb{E}_{i}\left[\sum_{j=i}^{\infty}(\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j})\right].

(116)

It is then easy to verify that on one hand, the norm of
\bm{U}_{i}
is uniformly bounded due to the exponential convergence of the Markov chain. Specifically, since for any positive integers
i<j
, it can be guaranteed that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{i}[\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j}]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbb{E}_{s_{j-1}\sim P^{j-i-1}(\cdot\mid s_{i}),s_{j}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{j-1})}[\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j}]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbb{E}_{s_{j-1}\sim P^{j-i-1}(\cdot\mid s_{i}),s_{j}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{j-1})}[\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j}]-\mathbb{E}_{s_{j-1}\sim\mu,s_{j}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{j-1})}[\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j}]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq d_{\mathsf{TV}}(P^{j-i-1}(\cdot\mid s_{i}),\mu)\cdot\sup_{s_{j-1},s_{j}}\|\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq m\rho^{j-i-1}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1).

Therefore, the norm of
\bm{U}_{i}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{i}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i}\|_{2}+\sum_{j=i+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{i}\|\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left(1+\sum_{j=i+1}^{\infty}m\rho^{j-i-1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{1-\rho}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1);

(117)

On the other hand,
\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i}
can be represented as

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i}[\bm{U}_{i+1}]

\displaystyle=(\bm{U}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}])+(\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}]-\mathbb{E}_{i}[\bm{U}_{i+1}])

\displaystyle=:\bm{m}_{i}+(\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}]-\mathbb{E}_{i}[\bm{U}_{i+1}])

(118)

Here, the first term
\bm{U}_{i}-\bm{U}_{i+1}
can be analyzed by the telescoping technique, while

\displaystyle\bm{m}_{i}:=\bm{U}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}]

(119)

is a martingale difference process. Furthermore, we observe that when
s_{0}
is drawn from the stationary distribution
\mu
, the covariance matrix
\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\bm{m}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}^{\top}\right]
is time-invariant, and can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\bm{m}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}^{\top}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bm{m}_{1}\bm{m}_{1}^{\top}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\bm{U}_{1}-\mathbb{E}_{0}[\bm{U}_{1}])(\bm{U}_{1}-\mathbb{E}_{0}[\bm{U}_{1}])^{\top}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bm{U}_{1}\bm{U}_{1}^{\top}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{0}[\bm{U}_{1}]\mathbb{E}_{0}[\bm{U}_{1}^{\top}]]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}[\bm{U}_{1}\bm{U}_{1}^{\top}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{1}[\bm{U}_{2}]\mathbb{E}_{1}[\bm{U}_{2}^{\top}]]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{1}[\bm{U}_{2}])(\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{1}[\bm{U}_{2}])^{\top}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{1}[\bm{U}_{2}]\mathbb{E}_{1}[\bm{U}_{2}^{\top}]]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1})(\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1})^{\top}]+\mathbb{E}[(\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1})\mathbb{E}_{1}[\bm{U}_{2}]^{\top}]+\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{1}[\bm{U}_{2}](\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1})^{\top}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1})(\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1})^{\top}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}[(\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1})(\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j})^{\top}+(\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j})(\bm{A}_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{1})^{\top}]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}},

(120)

according to the definition of
\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}
(as in eq. (
22
)). Notice here that we applied the rule of total expectation throughout this deduction, and took advantage of the time-invariant property of the distribution of
\{\bm{U}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq T}
in (i).

In order to relate
I_{2}
to the martingale difference process
\bm{m}_{i}
, we invoke the relation (
118
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}]-\mathbb{E}_{i}[\bm{U}_{i+1}])

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}(\bm{Q}_{i-1}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}]-\bm{Q}_{i}\mathbb{E}_{i}[\bm{U}_{i+1}])+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}(\bm{Q}_{i}-\bm{Q}_{i-1})\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}]

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{21}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}}}+\underset{I_{22}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{T}(\bm{Q}_{0}\mathbb{E}_{0}[\bm{U}_{1}]-\bm{Q}_{T}\mathbb{E}_{T}[\bm{U}_{T+1}])}}+\underset{I_{23}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}(\bm{Q}_{i}-\bm{Q}_{i-1})\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}]}}

where we applied the telescoping technique in the last equation. The uniform boundedness of
\|\bm{Q}_{t}\|
, as indicated by Lemma
A.11
, and the uniform boundedness of
\|\bm{U}_{i}\|_{2}
, as indicated by (
117
), guarantee that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{T}(\bm{Q}_{0}\mathbb{E}_{0}[\bm{U}_{1}]-\bm{Q}_{T}\mathbb{E}_{T}[\bm{U}_{T+1}])\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}(\|\bm{Q}_{0}\|\sup\|\bm{U}_{1}\|_{2}+\|\bm{Q}_{T}\|\sup\|\bm{U}_{T}\|_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\left(\frac{2m}{1-\rho}\right)(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

(121)

deterministically.
Meanwhile, the norm of
I_{23}
is bounded by invoking Lemma
A.15
:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}(\bm{Q}_{i}-\bm{Q}_{i-1})\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}]\right\|\lesssim\eta_{0}\left[\eta_{0}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\right)+\alpha\right]\left(\frac{1}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\left(\frac{2m}{1-\rho}\right)(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\frac{\log T}{T}

(122)

almost surely.

It now boils down to bounding the norm of
I_{21}
. Towards this end, we firstly observe that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{s_{i-1}\sim\mu,s_{i}\sim P(\cdot\mid s_{i-1})}\|\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}=\mathsf{Tr}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}),

and that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\|\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\eta_{0}\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\left(\frac{2m}{1-\rho}\right)(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

almost surely for all
i\in[T]
, according to Lemma
A.11
and Equation (
117
). Now consider a sequence of matrix-valued functions
\bm{F}_{i}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{R}^{(d+1)\times(d+1)}
, defined as

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{i}(s,s^{\prime})=\begin{pmatrix}0&(\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}(s,s^{\prime}))^{\top}\\
\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}(s,s^{\prime})&\bm{0}_{d\times d}.\end{pmatrix},\quad\forall i\in[T].

It can then be verified that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\mu,s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid s)}[\bm{F}_{i}^{2}(s,s^{\prime})]\right\|=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\mu,s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid s)}\|\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}(s,s^{\prime})\|_{2}^{2},

and that

\displaystyle\|\bm{F}_{i}(s,s^{\prime})\|=\|\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}(s,s^{\prime})\|_{2},\quad\forall s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}.

Therefore, a direct application of Corollary
3.1
yields

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2\sqrt{\frac{2\mathsf{Tr}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T})}{T}\log\frac{12d}{\delta}}

\displaystyle+\eta_{0}\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\left(\frac{2m}{1-\rho}\right)(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)(1-\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\frac{1}{T}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{6d}{\delta},

(123)

with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{3}
.

Bounding
I_{3}

Applying a similar technique as in the proof of Theorem
C.3
, we decompose the term
I_{3}
as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}})+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{31}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}})}}+\underset{I_{32}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}])\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}}}+\underset{I_{33}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}]-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}}}.

(124)

Recall from the proof of Theorem
C.3
that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{\theta}_{i-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{1-\alpha}\eta_{i}(2\max_{1\leq j<i}\|\bm{\Delta}_{j}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1);

hence the norm of
I_{31}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\|\bm{Q}_{i}\|\cdot\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{1-\alpha}\eta_{i}(2\max_{1\leq j<i}\|\bm{\Delta}_{j}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{(1-\alpha)T}\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\eta_{i}(2\max_{1\leq j<T}\|\bm{\Delta}_{j}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{0}}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}(2\max_{1\leq j<T}\|\bm{\Delta}_{j}\|_{2}+2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)T^{-\alpha}.

(125)

The term
I_{32}
can be decomposed into
t_{\mathsf{mix}}
martingales and bounded by the vector Azuma’s inequality, invoking a similar technique to the tackling of the term
I_{4}
in the proof of Theorem
C.3
. With details omitted, we obtain with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{3}
that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}])\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\sqrt{\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{1-\alpha}\log\frac{9t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\delta}}R^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}}.

(126)

The term
I_{33}
is bounded by the mixing property of the Markov chain, specifically

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}]-\bm{A})\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{T}\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}R^{\prime}(i_{\mathsf{mix}})^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lesssim\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}R^{\prime}T^{-\alpha}.

(127)

Completing the proof

Combining (
123
), (
121
), (
122
), (
125
), (
126
), and (
127
) by a union bound argument and plugging in the definition of
R^{\prime}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2\sqrt{\frac{2\mathsf{Tr}(\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T})}{T}\log\frac{6d}{\delta}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta_{0}t_{\mathsf{mix}}(T^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}\right)^{-\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}T^{-\alpha}

\displaystyle+\eta_{0}\sqrt{\frac{2t_{\mathsf{mix}}(T^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})}{2\alpha-1}\log\frac{27Tt_{\mathsf{mix}}(T^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})}{\delta}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}\right)^{-\frac{2+\alpha}{2(1-\alpha)}}T^{-\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta_{0}t_{\mathsf{mix}}(T^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})}{\sqrt{(1-\alpha)(2\alpha-1)}}\log\frac{27Tt_{\mathsf{mix}}(T^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})}{\delta}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}\right)^{-\frac{2+\alpha}{2(1-\alpha)}}T^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\eta_{0}\frac{m}{1-\rho}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{4}\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}\right)^{-\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}T^{-1}

\displaystyle\cdot\left[(1-\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{6d}{\delta}+\left(\eta_{0}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\right)+\alpha\right)\log T\right].

Notice that all the terms beginning from the second line can all be bounded by

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}T^{-\alpha}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{dT}{\delta},

where
\widetilde{C}
is a problem-related quantity depending on
\alpha,\eta_{0},\lambda_{0},\gamma,m,\rho
and
\lambda
. The theorem follows immediately.

C.4
Proof of Theorem
4.3

Following the precedent of
Wu et al. [2024]
, we approach this Berry–Esseen bound by introducing a Gaussian comparison term. Specifically, the triangle inequality indicates

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}(\sqrt{T}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))\leq d_{\mathsf{C}}(\sqrt{T}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}))+d_{\mathsf{C}}(\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}),\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))

(128)

where the second term on the right-hand-side can be bounded by the following proposition.

Lemma C.4
.

With
\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}
defined as in (
21
) and (
46
) respectively, it can be guaranteed for any
\eta_{0}\leq\frac{1}{2\lambda_{\Sigma}}
that

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}(\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}),\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{d\mathsf{cond}(\bm{\widetilde{\Gamma}})}}{(1-\gamma)\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}}T^{\alpha-1}+O(T^{2\alpha-2}).

Proof.

This lemma is a direct generalization of Theorem 3.3 in
Wu et al. [2024]
, where
\overline{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}
is replaced by
\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}
and
\bm{\Lambda}^{\star}
is replaced by
\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}
.
∎

We next focus on the first term on the right-hand-side of (
128
). For this, we notice that

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}(\sqrt{T}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}))=d_{\mathsf{C}}(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}\bm{A}^{\top}));

and we will focus on bounding the latter.

Recall that
\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}
can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{1}}{\underbrace{\frac{\bm{A}}{\sqrt{T}\eta_{0}}\bm{Q}_{0}\bm{\Delta}_{0}}}-\underset{I_{2}}{\underbrace{\frac{\bm{A}}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}}}-\underset{I_{3}}{\underbrace{\frac{\bm{A}}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{Q}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})}}.

(129)

In order to derive the non-asymptotic rate at which
\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}
converges to its Gaussian distribution, we derive the convergence of
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
and
I_{3}
accordingly in the following paragraphs. For readability concerns, we will only keep track of dependence on
T
and
d
in this proof, and use
\widetilde{C}
to denote any problem-related parameters that are related to
\alpha,\gamma,\eta_{0},\lambda_{0},m,\rho
.

The
a.s.
convergence of
I_{1}

Lemma
A.11
directly implies that as
T\to\infty
,
I_{1}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\bm{A}}{\sqrt{T}\eta_{0}}\bm{Q}_{0}\bm{\Delta}_{0}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}\eta_{0}}\|\bm{A}\bm{Q}_{0}\|\|\bm{\Delta}_{0}\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{2}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}T^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(130)

almost surely.

Bounding
I_{2}
with high probability

The convergence of
I_{2}
is result of the uniform boundedness of
\bm{Q}_{i}
, the convergence of
\{\bm{\Delta}_{t}\}
, and the mixing property of the Markov chain. Specifically, we again apply the technique in the proof of Theorem
C.3
and define

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{mix}}=t_{\mathsf{mix}}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),\quad\text{and}\quad i_{\mathsf{mix}}=\max\left\{i-t_{\mathsf{mix}},0\right\}.

(131)

Assumption
4.1
implies that (see
87
)

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{mix}}\leq\frac{\log m+\frac{1}{2}\log T}{\log(1/\rho)}\lesssim\frac{\log T}{1-\rho}.

(132)

The term
I_{2}
can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\Delta}_{i}-\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{21}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})(\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}})}}+\underset{I_{22}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}])\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{I_{23}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}]-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}}};

for the term
I_{21}
, recall that the difference between
\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}
and
\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}
can be further decomposed into

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}-\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}=\bm{\theta}_{i-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\eta_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{j}-\bm{\theta}_{j-1})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\eta_{j}(\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}_{j-1}-\bm{b}_{j})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\eta_{j}(\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j})-\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\eta_{j}\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\Delta}_{j-1}.

Hence, the decomposition of
I_{2}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=-\underset{I_{20}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\left[\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\eta_{j}(\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{j})\right]}}

\displaystyle-\underset{I_{21}^{\prime}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\left[\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\eta_{j}\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\Delta}_{j-1}\right]}}+I_{22}+I_{23},

(133)

where the norm of
I_{20}
is bounded almost surely by

\displaystyle\|I_{20}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\|\bm{AQ}_{i}\|\|\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A}\|\cdot\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\eta_{j}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}(2+\widetilde{C}i^{\alpha-1})\cdot t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{i}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\left[\frac{\eta_{0}}{1-\rho}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\log T+\widetilde{C}T^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}T\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{0}}{1-\rho}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}).

(134)

Here, the second line follows from Lemma
A.11
, and the third line uses the bound (
132
) on
t_{\mathsf{mix}}
.

For the term
I_{21}^{\prime}
, we invoke the fact that for any vectors
\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, it can be guaranteed that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{x}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq n\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2};

Consequently, the norm of
I_{21}^{\prime}
is bounded, in expectation, by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|I_{21}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{T}\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\eta_{j}\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\Delta}_{j-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\cdot(Tt_{\mathsf{mix}})\sum_{i=1}^{T}\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A})\eta_{j}\bm{A}_{j}\bm{\Delta}_{j-1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq t_{\mathsf{mix}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\left\|\bm{AQ}_{i}\right\|^{2}\|\bm{A}_{i}-\bm{A}\|^{2}\cdot\left(4\sum_{j=i_{\mathsf{mix}}+1}^{i-1}\eta_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{j-1}\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta_{0}^{2}t_{\mathsf{mix}}^{2}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{T}(2+\widetilde{C}i^{\alpha-1})i^{-2\alpha}\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{2}}i^{-\alpha}\log^{2}i+\widetilde{C}^{\prime}i^{-1}\log^{2}i\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{C}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}T^{1-3\alpha}\log^{4}T=o(T^{\frac{3}{2}-3\alpha}),

(135)

where we invoke Theorem
C.1
in the fourth line.

The term
I_{22}
can be decomposed into
t_{\mathsf{mix}}
martingales:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}])\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\left\|\sum_{r=1}^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sum_{i=0}^{T^{\prime}-1}\bm{AQ}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}(\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}-\mathbb{E}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}[\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}])\bm{\Delta}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{T}\sum_{r=1}^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{T^{\prime}-1}\bm{AQ}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}(\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}-\mathbb{E}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}[\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}])\bm{\Delta}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}\right\|.

Notice here that for any
r\in[t_{\mathsf{mix}}]
, the sequence

\displaystyle\left\{\bm{AQ}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}(\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}-\mathbb{E}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}[\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}])\bm{\Delta}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}\right\}_{i=0}^{T^{\prime}-1}

is a martingale difference. Therefore, its expected norm can be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{T^{\prime}-1}\bm{AQ}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}(\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}-\mathbb{E}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}[\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}])\bm{\Delta}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{T^{\prime}-1}\mathbb{E}\left\|\bm{AQ}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}(\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}-\mathbb{E}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}[\bm{A}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}])\bm{\Delta}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{T^{\prime}-1}\|\bm{AQ}_{it_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{(i-1)t_{\mathsf{mix}}+r}\|_{2}^{2}

Therefore, the norm of
I_{22}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}])\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\|\bm{AQ}_{i}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\log T}{(1-\rho)T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}(2+O(i^{\alpha-1}))^{2}\left[\frac{\eta_{0}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{2}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}i_{\mathsf{mix}}^{-\alpha}\log^{2}i+O(i_{\mathsf{mix}}^{-1}\log^{2}i)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta_{0}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}T^{-\alpha}\log^{3}T+O(T^{-1}\log^{3}T)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{0}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}T^{-\alpha}\log^{3}T+o(T^{-\alpha}).

(136)

For
I_{23}
, we make use of the fact that since

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\mathsf{TV}}(P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(\cdot\mid s),\mu)\leq T^{-1/2},

the difference between
\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}]
and
\bm{A}=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\bm{A}_{i}]
is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}]-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\bm{A}_{i}]\right\|\leq\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}d_{\mathsf{TV}}(P^{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(\cdot\mid s),\mu)\cdot\sup_{s_{i-1},s_{i}}\|\bm{A}_{i}\|\leq 2T^{-1/2}.

Hence, by AM-GM inequality, the expected norm of
I_{23}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}]-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left\|\bm{AQ}_{i}(\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}]-\bm{A})\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left\{\|\bm{AQ}_{i}\|^{2}\|\mathbb{E}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}[\bm{A}_{i}]-\bm{A}\|^{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{T}(2+O(i^{\alpha-1}))(T^{-1})\left[\frac{\eta_{0}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{2}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}i_{\mathsf{mix}}^{-\alpha}\log^{2}i+O(i_{\mathsf{mix}}^{-1}\log^{2}i)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\eta_{0}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{2}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}T^{-\alpha}\log^{2}T+O(T^{-1}\log^{2}T)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{0}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{2}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}T^{-\alpha}\log^{2}T+o(T^{-\alpha}).

(137)

Combining (
133
), (
134
), (
135
), (
136
) and (
137
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|I_{2}-\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}T^{\frac{1-2\alpha}{2}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|I_{2}-I_{20}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(\mathbb{E}\|I_{21}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|I_{22}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|I_{23}\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime})^{3}T^{-\alpha}\log^{3}T+o(T^{\frac{3}{2}-3\alpha}+T^{-\alpha}),

where we use
\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}
to denote problem-related quantities

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{\eta_{0}}{1-\rho}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1),\quad\text{and}

(138)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{1-\rho}\left(\frac{\eta_{0}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}.

(139)

Therefore, the Chebyshev’s inequality directly implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|I_{2}-\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\log T-o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha})\right\|_{2}\gtrsim\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}).

Applying the triangle inequality, we obtain the bound on
I_{2}
with high probability by triangle inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|I_{2}\right\|_{2}\gtrsim\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\log T+\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}})\right)\lesssim\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}).

(140)

A Berry–Esseen bound for
I_{3}

Following the decomposition of the term
I_{2}
in the proof of Theorem
4.2
, we represent
I_{3}
as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{31}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}}}+\underset{I_{32}}{\underbrace{\frac{\bm{A}}{\sqrt{T}}(\bm{Q}_{0}\mathbb{E}_{0}[\bm{U}_{1}]-\bm{Q}_{T}\mathbb{E}_{T}[\bm{U}_{T+1}])}}+\underset{I_{33}}{\underbrace{\frac{\bm{A}}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}(\bm{Q}_{i}-\bm{Q}_{i-1})\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\bm{U}_{i}]}}

where
\bm{U}_{i}
is defined as in (
116
) and
\bm{m}_{i}
is defined as in (
119
). Here, the norm of
I_{32}
and
I_{33}
can be bounded by
O(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})
almost surely; it now boils down to the term
I_{31}
, for which we aim to apply Corollary
3.2
. Specifically, let

\displaystyle\bm{f}_{i}(s_{i},s_{i-1})=\bm{AQ}_{i}\bm{m}_{i},

it is easy to verify that for all
i\in[T]
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{f}_{i}(s_{i},s_{i-1})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{A}\bm{Q}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{AQ}_{i}\|\|\bm{m}_{i}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(2+O(i^{\alpha-1}))\cdot\frac{m}{1-\rho}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta_{0}\left(\frac{1}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\frac{m}{1-\rho}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1),\quad\text{a.s.}

Meanwhile,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\bm{f}_{i}\bm{f}_{i}^{\top}]=\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}\bm{A}^{\top},\quad\text{with}

\displaystyle\|\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}\bm{A}^{\top}-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}\|\leq O(T^{\alpha-1}).

and when
T
satisfies (
48
), it can be guaranteed that
\lambda_{\min}(\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}\bm{A}^{\top})\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}})
. Hence, a direct application of Corollary
3.2
reveals that

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}\bm{m}_{i},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}\bm{A}^{\top})\right)\leq\widetilde{C}_{3}T^{-\frac{1}{4}}\log T+o(T^{-\frac{1}{4}}),

(141)

where
\widetilde{C}_{3}
is a problem-related quantity

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{3}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\{\left(\frac{p}{(p-1)(1-\lambda)}\log\left(d\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right\|_{\mu,p}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot\frac{m}{1-\rho}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{m}{1-\rho}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)}\cdot\eta_{0}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2(1-\alpha)}}\log^{\frac{1}{4}}(d\|\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}\|)\Bigg\}\cdot\sqrt{d}\|\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(142)

Completing the proof

The proof now boils down to combining the convergence rate of
I_{1},I_{2}
and the Berry–Esseen bound on
I_{3}
. For simplicity, we denote

\displaystyle\bm{\delta}_{T}:=\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}{\bm{m}}_{i}=I_{1}-I_{2}-I_{32}-I_{33}.

From the previous calculations, we have shown that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\bm{\delta_{T}}\right\|_{2}\gtrsim\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\log T+\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}})\right)\lesssim\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}),

and that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathcal{A}\in\mathscr{C}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}{\bm{AQ}_{i}\bm{m}}_{i}\in\mathcal{A}\right)-\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}\in\mathcal{A})\right|\leq\widetilde{C}_{3}T^{-\frac{1}{4}}{\log T}+o(T^{-\frac{1}{4}}).

We now combine these two results to bound the difference between the distributions of
\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}
and
\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}\bm{A}^{\top})
. Considering any convex set
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}:=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\inf_{y\in\mathcal{A}}\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon\},\quad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{A}^{-\varepsilon}:=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:B(\bm{x},\varepsilon)\subset\mathcal{A}\}.

Direct calculation yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\in\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\in\mathcal{A},\|\bm{\delta}_{T}\|_{2}>\varepsilon)+\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\in\mathcal{A},\|\bm{\delta}_{T}\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{\delta}_{T}\|_{2}>\varepsilon)+\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\in\mathcal{A},\|\bm{\delta}_{T}\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon).

Here, the triangle inequality implies

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\in\mathcal{A},\|\bm{\delta}_{T}\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon\right)\Rightarrow\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\bm{AQ}_{i}{\bm{m}}_{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}.

Hence,
\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\in\mathcal{A})
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\in\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{\delta}_{T}\|_{2}>\varepsilon)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{T}{\bm{AQ}_{i}\bm{m}}_{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{\delta}_{T}\|_{2}>\varepsilon)+\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}\in\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}\right)+\widetilde{C}_{3}T^{-\frac{1}{4}}{\log T}+o(T^{-\frac{1}{4}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{\delta}_{T}\|_{2}>\varepsilon)+\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}\in\mathcal{A}\right)+\|\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon+\widetilde{C}_{3}T^{-\frac{1}{4}}{\log T}+o(T^{-\frac{1}{4}}),

where we invoked Theorem
A.17
in the last inequality. By letting

\displaystyle\varepsilon\asymp\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\log T+\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}),

we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T}\in\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}})+\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}\in\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle+\|\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\log T+\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\log T+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}})\right)+\widetilde{C}_{3}T^{-\frac{1}{4}}{\log T}+o(T^{-\frac{1}{4}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}\in\mathcal{A}\right)+(\widetilde{C}_{1}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+\widetilde{C}_{2}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}+\widetilde{C}_{3}T^{-\frac{1}{4}}){\log T}+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}+T^{-\frac{1}{4}}).

Here, in the last equality, we denote, for simplicity,

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{1}=\|\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}=\|\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\eta_{0}}{1-\rho}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1),\quad\text{and}

(143)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{2}=(\|\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+1)\widetilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}=(\|\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+1)\frac{1}{1-\rho}\left(\frac{\eta_{0}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}}{\lambda_{0}(1-\gamma)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}.

(144)

Using the same technique as in the proof of Corollary
3.2
, a lower bound can be derived symmetrically. By taking a supremum over
\mathcal{A}\in\mathscr{C}
, it can be guaranteed that

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}(\sqrt{T}\bm{A}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{A}\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}\bm{A}^{\top}))

\displaystyle\lesssim(\widetilde{C}_{1}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+\widetilde{C}_{2}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}+\widetilde{C}_{3}T^{-\frac{1}{4}}){\log T}+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}+T^{-\frac{1}{4}}).

(145)

Further combining (
145
) with (
128
) and Lemma
C.4
, we obtain the Berry–Esseen bound

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}(\sqrt{T}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))

\displaystyle\lesssim(\widetilde{C}_{1}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+\widetilde{C}_{2}T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}+\widetilde{C}_{3}T^{-\frac{1}{4}}+\widetilde{C}_{4}T^{\alpha-1}){\log T}

\displaystyle+o(T^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+T^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}+T^{-\frac{1}{4}}+T^{\alpha-1})

with
\widetilde{C}_{1}
,
\widetilde{C}_{2}
,
\widetilde{C}_{3}
defined as in (
143
), (
144
), (
142
) respectively, and

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{4}=\frac{\sqrt{d\mathsf{cond}(\bm{\widetilde{\Gamma}})}}{(1-\gamma)\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}}.

Finally, when
\alpha=\frac{3}{4}
, we have, coincidentally,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}-\alpha=-\frac{\alpha}{3}=-\frac{1}{4}=\alpha-1.

Hence, Theorem
4.3
follows from taking
\widetilde{C}=\max\{\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2},\widetilde{C}_{3},\widetilde{C}_{4}\}
.

C.5
Proof of Relation (
25
)

Following the same logic as Appendix B.4.1 in
Wu et al. [2024]
, we can obtain a lower bound on the difference between
\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T})
and
\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star})
. Specifically, when
T
is sufficiently large,

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}(\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}),\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))=d_{\mathsf{TV}}(\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}),\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))\gtrsim O(T^{\alpha-1}).

Meanwhile, when
\alpha>\frac{3}{4}
, both
\frac{1}{2}-\alpha
and
-\frac{\alpha}{3}
are less than
-\frac{1}{4}
. Therefore, the upper bound (
145
) is transformed as

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}(\sqrt{T}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}))\leq O(T^{-\frac{1}{4}}).

In combination, the triangle inequality reveals

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{C}}(\sqrt{T}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))

\displaystyle\geq d_{\mathsf{C}}(\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}),\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\star}))-d_{\mathsf{C}}(\sqrt{T}\overline{\bm{\Delta}}_{T},\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}_{T}))

\displaystyle\gtrsim O(T^{\alpha-1})-O(T^{-\frac{1}{4}})

\displaystyle\gtrsim O(T^{\alpha-1})\gtrsim O(T^{-\frac{1}{4}}).

Here, in the last line, we applied the fact that since
\alpha>\frac{3}{4}
,
\alpha-1\geq-\frac{1}{4}
.

Choice of stepsizes

We conclude by noting that choice of the stepsize in Theorem
4.2
and Theorem
4.3
are different: in Theorem
4.2
,
\eta_{0}
depends on
\delta
and other problem-related quantities like
\lambda_{0}
and
\gamma
, and
\alpha
can take any value between
\frac{1}{2}
and
1
; however, in Theorem
4.3
, the initial stepsize
\eta_{0}
can take any value less than
1/2\lambda_{\Sigma}
, while
\alpha
is set to the specific value of
\frac{3}{4}
. In fact, our proof of Theorem
4.3
allows for a general choice of
\alpha
; however, using other values of
\alpha
other than
3/4
appears to be suboptimal.

Appendix D
Proof of supportive lemmas and propositions

D.1
Proof of Proposition
B.1

We address these recursive relations in order.

Proof of (
57
).

By definition and according to Proposition
A.3
, the left-hand-side is featured by

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\parallel}\right)^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\parallel}\right)^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\parallel}\right]=\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\exp(t\bm{H})]\right\|\cdot\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|.

Since
\mu(\bm{H})=\bm{0}
, the expectation of
\exp(t\bm{H})
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\exp(t\bm{H})\right\|=\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\frac{1}{k!}(t\bm{H})^{k}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{I}\|+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(t\bm{H})^{2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}(tM)^{k}=\exp(tM)-tM.

Proof of (
58
).

Since
\bm{g}^{\parallel}(x)=\mu(\bm{g})=\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}
is a constant function, the left hand side can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\parallel}\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}\cdot\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}=\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}\cdot\left\|\mathcal{P}^{*}\left(\exp(t\bm{H})\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}\cdot\lambda\left\|\left(\exp(t\bm{H})\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}\cdot\lambda\left\|\left(\exp(t\bm{H})-\bm{I}\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}\cdot\lambda\sup_{x\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\exp(t\bm{H}(x))-\bm{I}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{g}^{\parallel}\|_{\mu}\cdot\lambda(\exp(tM)-1).

Proof of (
59
).

By definition, the left-hand-side can be represented as

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\perp}\right)^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu}=\left\|\mu\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\perp}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\mu(\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\perp})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\mu((\exp(t\bm{H})-\bm{I})\bm{g}^{\perp})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\exp(t\bm{H})-\bm{I})\bm{g}^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\exp(t\bm{H})-\bm{I}\right\|\cdot\left\|\bm{g}^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq(\exp(tM)-1)\left\|\bm{g}^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}.

Proof of (
60
).

As a direct implication of Proposition
A.3
, the left-hand-side is featured by

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\perp}\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}=\left\|\mathcal{P}^{*}\left(\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\perp}\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\left(\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\perp}\right)^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\exp(t\bm{H})\bm{g}^{\perp}\right\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\sup_{x\in\mathcal{S}}\|\exp(t\bm{H}(x))\|\|\bm{g}^{\perp}\|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\exp(tM)\|\bm{g}^{\perp}\|_{\mu}.

D.2
Proof of Lemma
B.2

Direct calculation reveals

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{4}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{4})^{2}+4\alpha_{3}^{2}}}{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\frac{\sqrt{(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{4})^{2}+4\alpha_{3}^{2}}}{2}-\frac{\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{4}}{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\frac{2\alpha_{3}^{2}}{\sqrt{(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{4})^{2}+4\alpha_{3}^{2}}+(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{4})}.

(146)

In what follows, we firstly illustrate that

\displaystyle\sqrt{(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{4})^{2}+4\alpha_{3}^{2}}+(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{4})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)^{2}+4(e^{x}-1)^{2}}+(1-\lambda)e^{x}-x

\displaystyle=:f(x)\geq 2(1-\lambda).

(147)

In fact, since
f(0)=2(1-\lambda)
, it suffices to show that
f^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
for all
x\geq 0
. Towards this end, observe that

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)((1-\lambda)e^{x}-1)+4(e^{x}-1)e^{x}}{\sqrt{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)^{2}+4(e^{x}-1)^{2}}}+(1-\lambda)e^{x}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{[(1-\lambda)e^{x}-1]\left[\sqrt{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)^{2}+4(e^{x}-1)^{2}}-((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)\right]+4(e^{x}-1)e^{x}}{\sqrt{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)^{2}+4(e^{x}-1)^{2}}}

Since the denominator is always positive, we now focus on showing that the numerator. Specifically, we discuss the following three cases:

1.

If
(1-\lambda)e^{x}-1\geq 0
, then since
\sqrt{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)^{2}+4(e^{x}-1)^{2}}>(1-\lambda)e^{x}-x
, the numerator is positive;

2.

If
(1-\lambda)e^{x}-1<0
and
(1-\lambda)e^{x}-x\geq 0
, then by triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sqrt{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)^{2}+4(e^{x}-1)^{2}}-((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)\leq 2(e^{x}-1).

Meanwhile, since
(1-\lambda)e^{x}-1>-1>-e^{x}
, it can be guaranteed that

\displaystyle[(1-\lambda)e^{x}-1]\left[\sqrt{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)^{2}+4(e^{x}-1)^{2}}-((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)\right]+4(e^{x}-1)e^{x}

\displaystyle>-e^{x}[2(e^{x}-1)]+4(e^{x}-1)e^{x}>0.

3.

If
(1-\lambda)e^{x}-1<0
and
(1-\lambda)e^{x}-x<0
, then also by triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sqrt{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)^{2}+4(e^{x}-1)^{2}}-((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)

\displaystyle\leq 2(e^{x}-1)+2(x-(1-\lambda)e^{x})

\displaystyle<2(e^{x}-1+x)<4(e^{x}-1).

Therefore, again because
(1-\lambda)e^{x}-1>-1>-e^{x}
, the numerator is bounded below by

\displaystyle[(1-\lambda)e^{x}-1]\left[\sqrt{((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)^{2}+4(e^{x}-1)^{2}}-((1-\lambda)e^{x}-x)\right]+4(e^{x}-1)e^{x}

\displaystyle>e^{-x}\cdot 4(e^{x}-1)+4(e^{x}-1)e^{x}>0.

In all three cases, we have proved that
f^{\prime}(x)>0
. This complete the proof of (
147
). As a direct consequence of (
146
) and (
147
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{4}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{4})^{2}+4\alpha_{3}^{2}}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{1}+\frac{\alpha_{3}^{2}}{1-\lambda}=(e^{x}-x)+\frac{(e^{x}-1)^{2}}{1-\lambda}.

We now proceed to further bound this upper bound. On one hand, when
x\in(0,1)
, It can be guaranteed that
e^{x}-x<1+x^{2}
and
e^{x}-1<2x
. Therefore,

\displaystyle(e^{x}-x)+\frac{(e^{x}-1)^{2}}{1-\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq 1+x^{2}+\frac{(2x)^{2}}{1-\lambda}

\displaystyle<1+\frac{5x^{2}}{1-\lambda}<\exp\left(\frac{5x^{2}}{1-\lambda}\right)

where we invoked the fact that
1+x<e^{x}
in the last inequality. On the other hand, when
x>1
, define

\displaystyle g(x)=\exp\left(\frac{5x^{2}}{1-\lambda}\right)-\left[(e^{x}-x)+\frac{(e^{x}-1)^{2}}{1-\lambda}\right];

it is easy to illustrate that
g^{\prime}(x)>0
for any
x>1
, and therefore
g(x)
is monotonically increasing with respect to
x
. This completes the proof of the Lemma.

D.3
Proof of Proposition
B.3

This proposition is a generalization of Proposition 2.2 in
Gallouët et al. [2018]
, and the proofs are similar to each other. Recall from
Gallouët et al. [2018]
, proof of Proposition 2.2, that for any
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|\bm{\alpha}\|=1
,

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{x})\bm{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right]\mathrm{d}t.

Hence, the difference between
\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{x})
and
\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{y})
can be featured by

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{x})-\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{y}))\bm{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)\left(g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})-g(\sqrt{t}\bm{y}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\underset{I_{1}}{\underbrace{\int_{0}^{1-\eta}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)\left(g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})-g(\sqrt{t}\bm{y}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t}}

\displaystyle-\underset{I_{2}}{\underbrace{\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right]\mathrm{d}t}}

\displaystyle+\underset{I_{3}}{\underbrace{\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)g(\sqrt{t}\bm{y}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right]\mathrm{d}t}},

(148)

where
\eta\in(0,1]
is a variable to be determined later. We address the terms
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
and
I_{3}
accordingly.

Bounding
I_{1}
.

Since
g(\bm{x})=h(\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}+\bm{\mu})
and
h\in\mathsf{Lip}_{1}
, it can be guaranteed that

\displaystyle\left|g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})-g(\sqrt{t}\bm{y}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right|\leq\sqrt{t}\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\right\|_{2};

hence,
I_{1}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{1-\eta}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)\left(g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})-g(\sqrt{t}\bm{y}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}\left|(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1\right|\int_{0}^{1-\eta}\frac{\sqrt{t}}{2(1-t)}\mathrm{d}t.

Here, since
\bm{z}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{d})
and
\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{2}=1
, we have
\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and therefore
(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}\sim\chi^{2}(1)
. Consequently,
\mathbb{E}\left|(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1\right|
is the standard error of
\chi^{2}(1)
distribution, thus a universal constant. Meanwhile, the integral is bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\eta}\frac{\sqrt{t}}{2(1-t)}\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{0}^{1-\eta}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathrm{d}t=-\frac{1}{2}(\log\eta).

In combination, the term
I_{1}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{1-\eta}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)\left(g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})-g(\sqrt{t}\bm{y}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\right\|_{2}(-\log\eta).

(149)

Bounding
I_{2}
.

Since
(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}\sim\chi^{2}(1)
, we naturally have
\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}]=1
. Therefore,
I_{2}
can be rephrased as

\displaystyle\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right]\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)\left(g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})-g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t,

and its absolute value can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right]\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[|(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1|\left|g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})-g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x})\right|\right]\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[|(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1|\sqrt{1-t}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{z}\|_{2}\right]\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbb{E}[|(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1|\|\bm{z}\|_{2}]\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\mathrm{d}t

As is illustrated by Equation (26) in
Gallouët et al. [2018]
, the expectation is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1|\|\bm{z}\|_{2}]\lesssim\sqrt{d},

and the integral is bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\mathrm{d}t\leq\sqrt{\eta}.

So in combination, the term
I_{2}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\int_{1-\eta}^{1}\frac{1}{2(1-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[((\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{z})^{2}-1)g(\sqrt{t}\bm{x}+\sqrt{1-t}\bm{z})\right]\mathrm{d}t\right|

(150)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{d}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sqrt{\eta}.

(151)

The term
I_{3}
can be bounded by a similar manner.

Completing the proof.

Combining (
148
), (
149
) and (
150
) by triangle inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{x})-\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{y}))\bm{\alpha}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\right\|_{2}(-\log\eta)+\sqrt{d}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sqrt{\eta}.

In the case where
2\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\|_{2}>\sqrt{d}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{2}
, we can simply take
\eta=1
, yielding the bound

\displaystyle\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{x})-\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{y}))\bm{\alpha}\right|\lesssim\sqrt{d}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|;

(152)

otherwise, when
2\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{d}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|
, we can set

\displaystyle\eta=\frac{4\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\|_{2}^{2}}{d\|\bm{\Sigma}\|},

yielding the bound

\displaystyle\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{x})-\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{y}))\bm{\alpha}\right|\lesssim 2\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\|_{2}\left(1+\log\frac{\sqrt{d}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|}{\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\|_{2}}\right).

(153)

For simplicity, we use
f(x,a)
to denote the piecewise function

\displaystyle f(x,a)=\begin{cases}&x+x\log a-x\log x,\quad\text{if }x\in[0,a];\\
&a,\quad\text{if }x>a.\end{cases}

It is easy to illustrate that

\displaystyle f(x)\leq(1+\log a)^{+}x+e^{-1}.

Therefore, by combining (
152
) and (
153
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{x})-\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{y}))\bm{\alpha}\right|

\displaystyle\leq f(2\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\|_{2},\sqrt{d}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|)

\displaystyle\leq(2+\log(d\|\bm{\Sigma}\|))^{+}\cdot\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\|_{2}+e^{-1}.

D.4
Proof of Equation (
72
)

Essentially, it suffices to show that for any fixed matrix
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and random vector
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Ax}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{x}\|_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Ax}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{x}\|_{2}.

(154)

To see this, we use

\displaystyle\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A}=\bm{PDP}^{\top}

to denote the eigen decomposition of
\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A}
, where
\bm{P}
is a ortho-normal matrix and
\bm{D}=\text{diag}\{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d}\}
where
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\ldots\geq\lambda_{d}\geq 0
. Further denote
\bm{y}=\bm{Px}
, then the norms of
\bm{x}
and
\bm{Ax}
can be represented by

\displaystyle\|\bm{Ax}\|_{2}^{2}=\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{PDP}^{\top}\bm{x}=\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{Dy}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}y_{i}^{2},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\|\bm{x}\|_{2}=\|\bm{y}\|_{2}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{d}y_{i}^{2}}.

For every
i\in[d]
, it is easy to verify that

\displaystyle y_{i}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\bm{y}\|_{2}

are positively correlated, and therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Ax}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{x}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}y_{i}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\|\bm{y}\|_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\mathbb{E}[y_{i}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\|\bm{y}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\mathbb{E}[y_{i}^{2}\|\bm{y}\|_{2}]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}y_{i}^{2}\right)\cdot\|\bm{y}\|_{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Ax}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{x}\|_{2}.

Here, the inequality on the third line follows from the Chebyshev’s association inequality.

D.5
Proof of Lemma
C.2

The TD update rule (
4.1
) directly implies that

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}(\bm{\theta}_{i+1}-\bm{\theta}_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}_{i}-\bm{b}_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})+\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{i}.

(155)

We will apply this relation to prove the three bounds respectively.

Proof of Equation (
88a
).

By triangle inequality, (
155
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}\mathbb{E}\|(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})\|_{2}+\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)+\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}2\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta^{\star}}\|_{2}+1)+2\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2},

where the last line follows from the fact that the stepsizes
\{\eta_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
are non-increasing.

Proof of Equation (
88b
).

We firstly notice that for a set of
n
vectors
\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}
, it always holds true that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{x}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq n\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore, (
155
) implies the following bound for
\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2t_{\mathsf{mix}}\cdot\left\{\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|(\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i})\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2t_{\mathsf{mix}}\cdot\left\{t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}+4\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=2t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{2}\left[t_{\mathsf{mix}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)^{2}+4\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right].

Proof of Equation (
88c
).

By triangle inequality, (
155
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}\|\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}\|\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\theta}^{\star}-\bm{b}_{i}\|_{2}]+\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}\|\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}+\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\eta_{i}\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{A}_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}]

\displaystyle\leq t_{\mathsf{mix}}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}(2\|\bm{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}+1)\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\left(\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\Delta}_{t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}\|_{2}^{2}+2\sum_{i=t-t_{\mathsf{mix}}}^{t-1}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right).

This completes the proof of the lemma.
